# Approved rules ledger — every rule needs the operator's explicit yes (2026-10-01)

A cite `research/NN` or `review/…` below means that file under `docs/archive/` at git tag `design-archive-2026-11` (`git show design-archive-2026-11:docs/archive/research/<file>`). A source cell that ends "see moved sources" points at [Moved sources](#moved-sources) at the end of this file. The reference format is [reference-format.md](reference-format.md).

`reason` column (G21 #91, skills #98, operator 2026-10-17): "because" + the failure the rule prevents, drafted from the rule's own source or evidence; the cite each reason came from is in `review/reasons.json`. `NO-REASON` = no failure found in the cited sources, flagged for deletion (checklist #15). Empty = not an author rule (file fate, pointer, placement decision).

`evidence` column (G23 #93 Q3, skills #99, operator 2026-10-18): the Aruvii `path:lines @sha` that showed the mistake behind the rule's Good/Bad pair, and the neutral pair that replaces it in references (`research/68a`–`68f`). Ledger only — never reference text. The Aruvii originals stay in research/31a–31e-v2, 42, 47, 56a–c.

| GB1a | every reference (Good/Bad) | A Good example obeys EVERY approved rule and house style, not only its own; where no real example qualifies it is rewritten from the real one and labelled "adapted from path:lines" (JUnit Goods become Spock). | | operator feedback on the review page, 2026-10-02 | | SUPERSEDED by GB2 (the 'obeys every rule' half carries over into GB2) |
| SRC1 | every reference (Rules) | Every rule cites the reference text it comes from, read at path:line (reference project, its internal references, or a primary doc). Our code, CLAUDE.md and our docs are never a rule's source — they are only evidence of where the rule is broken. A rule no reference states is tagged "project decision" and cites the operator decision, ADR or ticket that made it. | because our own code does not follow the rules correctly | operator 2026-10-03 (#33 session): "never take reference from our code for a rule because it is not followed correctly" | | AMENDED by SRC2 (the "project decision" tag becomes the preference label) |
| GB2 | every reference (Good/Bad) | Bad = our real code, copied with path:lines at a pinned commit. Good = that same Bad rewritten to obey its rule and every other approved rule and house style, shaped like the reference's own example where one exists, labelled "rewritten from path:lines" and marked NEEDS COMPILE CHECK until work item 1 compiles it. Our code is never a Good. ## Code + observability (#33, G9b) — candidates research/29-java-standards.md §4; operator 2026-10-02

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| JV1 | code/references/java.md | Compute time in `java.time` types (`Instant`, `Duration`), never `Date`, `Calendar` or a raw `long` of millis; convert only at a library or display edge. | because `Date` and its formatters are mutable and not thread-safe | Error Prone `JavaUtilDate`, `StronglyTypeTime`; Sonar S2143; OpenJDK `SimpleDateFormat` javadoc (research/48 §1 JV1); earlier sources: see moved sources | `aruvii-agents/…/application/PipelineExecutionService.java:49,54-58,68,79-80` @772736bf (Bad, real) · pair research/68f-neutral-code.md | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JV2 | code/references/java.md | Use sequential streams in request- or queue-serving code; never `parallelStream()` or `.parallel()`. | because parallel streams run on the one JVM-wide common pool, so one blocking task stalls every other user of it | OpenJDK `ForkJoinPool` javadoc; Effective Java Item 48 (research/48 §1 JV2; weakest keep); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JV3 | code/references/java.md | Keep `map`, `filter` and other stream lambdas free of I/O, logging and mutation, and do not use `peek` for work. | because the stream may skip those calls, so the effect silently does not happen | OpenJDK `java.util.stream` package summary; Effective Java Item 46; Sonar S3864 (research/48 §1 JV3); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JV4 | code/references/java.md | Give every `toMap` a merge function. | because `toMap` throws `IllegalStateException` at runtime on a duplicate key | OpenJDK `Collectors.toMap` javadoc (research/48 §0 decision V; §1 JV4); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (vendor docs, decision V, agent-factory#59) |
| JV5 | code/references/java.md | Use `var` only when the initializer names the type (constructor, factory, literal); otherwise write the type, and never pair `var` with `<>`. | because the reader cannot see the type and diamond infers `Object` | OpenJDK amber LVTI style guide G3, G6; Sonar S6212 (research/48 §1 JV5); earlier sources: see moved sources | `aruvii-api/…/controller/ProviderController.java:58-61` @772736bf (Bad, real) · pair research/68f-neutral-code.md | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59); decided edge added 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60, r49 §0): record pattern takes the component type or `_`, not `var` |
| JV6 | code/references/java.md | Use `Optional` only as a return type, never as a field, parameter or collection element. | because an `Optional` parameter has three states (null, empty, present) and every callee must check all three | OpenJDK `Optional` apiNote; Sonar S3553; Effective Java Item 55 (research/48 §1 JV6); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JV7 | code/references/java.md | Write a multi-line SQL, JSON or HTML string as a text block, never as `+`-joined literals with `\n`. | because escaped concatenation hides the text's shape and invites a missing newline | OpenJDK amber text-blocks guide G1; Sonar S6126 (research/48 §1 JV7); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JV8 | code/references/java.md | Open every I/O-backed `AutoCloseable` (stream, reader, connection) in a try-with-resources header. | because a resource not closed when an exception fires leaks until the process runs out of handles | OpenJDK `AutoCloseable` javadoc; Effective Java Item 9; Sonar S2093; Error Prone `MustBeClosedChecker` (research/48 §1 JV8); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JV9 | code/references/java.md | Put braces on every `if`, `else`, `for`, `do` and `while` body. | because a line added under a brace-less body looks guarded but always runs | Google Java Style §4.1.1; Sonar S121 (research/48 §1 JV9); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JV10 | code/references/java.md (decided edge) | No JPMS — module boundaries are Gradle modules + AT `module_dependency_allowlist` (reword "Maven"). | because Spring reflection needs `opens` almost everywhere, which cancels JPMS encapsulation | modern-java §10 vs pl 141 Ex8 | | APPROVED |
| JV11 | — | DROPPED as rules: whitespace (RJ:28), hand DCL (§7), JEP 513 (§9), gatherers/laziness (functional §4-5) — no line of code can break them. | | operator 2026-10-02 | | REJECTED |

## LLM testing (#133, G34) — candidates research/69 §8 (LT) and research/70 §6 (LS); review page review/llm-testing-rules.html

Operator 2026-10-31, group "Fake model": approve LT1–LT5; change LT6 as recommended. Group "Agent loop": approve
LT7–LT10; change LT11 as recommended. Group "Agent scenarios" (rules and pairs together): approve LS1–LS9;
change LS10 as recommended, recorded as an ours-rule (no reference source). Group "Recordings" (rules and pairs
together): approve all. Group "Live tests" (rules and pairs together): approve as recommended, with LS12, LS14,
LS17 and LS18 merged and LT14 changed. Group "Evals": approve all. Placement: two files, by tier —
`testing/references/llm-tests.md` (fake tier: LT1–LT13, LS1–LS10, and LS12 merged into LT6) and
`testing/references/llm-live-tests.md` (live tier and evals: LT14–LT18, LS11, LS13–LS20). Generic rules only: each project's
own choices (providers, which flows run live, the schedule) go in its `docs/agents/testing.md` (#135).

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LT1 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Fake the model behind your own model port with one shared, scripted fake. | because copied per-test mocks drift from the protocol and from each other | OA:src/agents/testing/model.py:249; ADKJ:core/src/test/java/com/google/adk/testing/TestLlm.java:47; contrast CA:tests/test_tool_callbacks.py:22, CA:tests/test_streaming_client.py:26 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT2 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Make the scripted fake fail on an unscripted extra call and on unused steps. | because a tolerant fake hides a loop that ran too many or too few turns | OA:…/model.py:487-496,313-319; ADKJ:…/TestLlm.java:197-207; SA:…/ScriptedChatModel.java:73-76 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT3 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Fail any test that reaches a real model client unless it opts in by name. | because an unguarded path spends money, runs slowly and flakes | OA:tests/conftest.py:129-141; PA:tests/conftest.py:107,580-582; PA:pydantic_ai_slim/pydantic_ai/models/__init__.py:2642-2643 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT4 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Assert on the request the code sent the model, not only on the final answer. | because lost tool results and broken history show up only in the next call's input | SA:…/SessionMemoryAdvisorToolLoopTests.java:49-60; OA:tests/test_agent_runner.py:2024-2037; LG:libs/prebuilt/tests/model.py:36; MA:…/aimock/README.md:15-19 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT5 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Test each provider adapter against a fake HTTP transport under the real SDK and assert the request JSON. | because a model-level fake skips the request mapping that breaks in production | L4J:…/MockHttpClient.java:28-30; ADKJ:core/src/test/java/com/google/adk/models/FakeGeminiApi.java:52,59; AJ:…/FakeHttpClient.kt:18-25 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT6 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Make every model adapter pass one shared contract suite, with each unsupported capability declared and tested to throw. | because an unchecked opt-out silently drops a parameter | L4J:…/AbstractBaseChatModelIT.java:679,1269-1286,1840; LG:libs/checkpoint-conformance/README.md:19 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED (change: merge with LS12 into one rule — the same contract suite runs at IT against a fake HTTP server and live against the provider) |
| LT7 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Feed tool and validation errors back to the model in tests and assert the run continues. | because a swallowed error stalls the loop or yields a confident wrong answer | AJ:…/BetaToolRunnerTest.kt:142-197,1189-1211; PA:tests/test_agent.py:342-420; ADKP:tests/unittests/flows/llm_flows/tools/test_functions_error_messages.py:103-128 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT8 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Cap the agent loop by turns or requests and test the cap. | because an unbounded tool loop turns one model misstep into a hung, billed run | AJ:…/BetaToolRunnerTest.kt:74; ADKJ:…/BaseLlmFlowTest.java:230-256; PA:tests/test_usage_limits.py:74-86; contrast SA:…/AnthropicChatModelIT.java:321-325 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT9 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | In streaming tests, script several chunks, assert they join to the final message and arrive in order, and bound every wait. | because streams lose, reorder or hang | L4J:…/AbstractBaseChatModelIT.java:193,727-757; L4J:…/AbstractStreamingChatModelIT.java:215; contrast SA:…/ScriptedChatModel.java:80-84 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT10 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | After an injected provider failure, assert the persisted conversation and side effects, not just the reply. | because a retry can store a partial reply or run a tool twice | MA:…/api-error-retry-rollback.scenario.test.ts:24,47-51,87-89; MA:…/approval-suspend-paths.scenario.test.ts:138-143 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT11 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Control time with a fixed clock and order races with explicit signals, never sleeps. | because timing-based tests stop exercising the regression and still pass | AJ:…/RetryingHttpClientTest.kt:195-227; LG:libs/langgraph/tests/test_retry.py:290-304; OA:tests/test_stream_input_guardrail_timing.py:185-260 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED (change: keep only "order races with explicit signals, never sleeps"; the fixed clock is already U1) |
| LS1 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Test the memory window with the real class, asserting the exact window after each message. | because evicting a tool call without its result makes the provider reject the next request | L4J:langchain4j/src/test/java/dev/langchain4j/memory/chat/MessageWindowChatMemoryTest.java:167,204; SA:spring-ai-model/…/MessageWindowChatMemoryTests.java:208,263 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS2 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Test compaction with a recording fake summariser: assert what is kept, what the summariser saw, and that a second pass never summarises again. | because repeated compaction rewrites history every turn | SA:spring-ai-session/…/ChatModelSummarizerTests.java:218; ADKJ:core/src/test/java/com/google/adk/summarizer/SlidingWindowEventCompactorTest.java:81,95 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS3 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Assert each request starts with the previous request's bytes while a fake clock moves. | because a timestamp or rewritten history silently busts the prompt cache | PA:tests/test_history_append_only.py:459; PA:tests/append_only.py:1247 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS4 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Assert cache markers sit only where the provider allows and no more than its limit. | because extra breakpoints waste the cache or fail the request | SA:models/spring-ai-anthropic/…/AnthropicChatModelTests.java:1042; SA:…/CacheEligibilityResolverTests.java:210 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS5 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Key a response cache on tenant, system prompt and inputs, and never cache an error. | because a key on user text alone serves one persona's answer to another | MA:…/agent-response-cache.test.ts:140,164,306; SA:…/SemanticCacheAdvisorTests.java:184 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS6 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | When a run crosses a usage or spend limit, keep the response that crossed it and count it. | because dropping it under-counts spend and re-requesting it bills twice | PA:tests/test_usage_limits.py:1216; PA:tests/harness/spend/test_spend.py:538,627 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS7 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Test that a blocking guard stops the run before the model or tool is called, with the rejection text as the tool result. | because a late guard lets side effects through | OA:tests/test_guardrails.py:423; OA:tests/test_agent_runner.py:2331 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS8 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Test approval by serialising the paused run, restoring it, then approving or rejecting, asserting each side effect ran exactly once. | because resume re-runs completed tools | OA:tests/test_resume_tool_use_behavior.py:15; ADKJ:…/RequestConfirmationLlmRequestProcessorTest.java:142,162 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS9 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Test fallback by failing the primary fake, then assert the serving model's name, the recorded failed attempt and the price on the serving model. | because cost reports book the wrong model | PA:tests/models/test_fallback.py:186,1992,228 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS10 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Test prompt injection arriving in a tool result through a whole run. | because every reference guards user input only, and tool results are where untrusted text enters | ours — no reference source (research/70 §2.12) | | APPROVED (ours-rule: no reference project tests this; closest is OA tests/test_tool_guardrails.py:351, called directly) |
| LT12 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | If you replay recorded traffic, fail on unplayed or unmatched interactions and never accept near-matches. | because a stale recording keeps passing after the code stops sending a field | PA:tests/conftest.py:847-875; PA:tests/AGENTS.md:15,159,163; contrast MA:packages/_llm-recorder/src/llm-recorder.ts:608,1019-1044 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LT13 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Scrub credentials, account ids and vendor headers in a pre-record hook, and pin load-bearing facts in the test body. | because recordings are committed forever and nobody reviews them | PA:tests/cassette_hooks.py:63-77,132-143,237-238; PA:tests/AGENTS.md:268; contrast MA:…/llm-recorder.ts:142-146 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LS11 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Live-test only what a fake cannot know — provider acceptance, real decisions, real chunking, real usage and errors — and keep the agent's own logic in fakes. | because live tests cost money every run | §3; §4 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS12 | testing/references/llm-tests.md | Write each live check once as a contract suite every provider adapter runs, with unsupported features declared and tested to fail. | because per-provider copies drift | L4J:L4J-C/AbstractBaseChatModelIT.java:1269-1286; SA:SA-T/AbstractToolCallingAdvisorIT.java:275 (research/70 §6) | | MERGED into LT6 (one contract suite, IT and live) |
| LS13 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | In a live test, assert a parameter's effect, not only acceptance (five output tokens give LENGTH; a stop sequence cuts text). | because a dropped field is accepted silently | L4J:L4J-C/AbstractBaseChatModelIT.java:396,527,1951 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS14 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Live-test tool calls on exact argument JSON and a marker the stub tool returns. | because that proves the model called the tool and our result reached it | L4J:L4J-C/AbstractBaseChatModelIT.java:706,786; SA:SA-A/AnthropicChatModelIT.java:326 (research/70 §6) | | MERGED into LT15 (its tool-call example) |

| LS16 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Live-test cache and usage numbers, and the price computed from them. | because only the provider reports cache-write and cache-read tokens | SA:SA-A/AnthropicPromptCachingIT.java:214; L4J:…/BedrockPromptCachingIT.java:383; PA:tests/models/test_anthropic.py:15157 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |
| LS17 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Check agent-level live tests on the state the run changed — a balance, a row, the routed agent — never on wording. | because wording varies by model and run | L4J:langchain4j-agentic/…/SupervisorAgentIT.java:312; ADKJ:contrib/langchain4j/…/LangChain4jIntegrationTest.java:306 (research/70 §6) | | MERGED into LT15 (its agent-outcome example) |
| LT14 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Run real-model tests in their own scheduled job with a pinned small model, a token cap and a protected secret, failing when skipped. | because skipped-everywhere tests rot unnoticed | PA:.github/workflows/gateway-model-health.yml:4-7,19-26; CA:e2e-tests/conftest.py:7-28; SA:.github/workflows/spring-ai-integration-tests.yml:4-28,111-117; OA:integration_tests/README.md:29 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED (change: no "scheduled"; per PR or nightly is the project's choice, #135; LS18 merged in) |
| LS18 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Pin a small model and a small token cap per live test, and fail when the key is missing. | because default models change and a skipped suite passes silently | CA:e2e-tests/conftest.py:16,24; SA:…/AnthropicChatOptions.java:76 (research/70 §6) | | MERGED into LT14 |
| LT15 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Assert real-model output only on what the test controls — structure, parsed fields, tool arguments, a sentinel or tool marker, side effects — because exact prose flakes and "not empty" catches nothing. | because  | MA:…/tool-calls-finish-reason.e2e.test.ts:149-159; L4J:…/AbstractBaseChatModelIT.java:706; ADKJ:…/LangChain4jIntegrationTest.java:159; OA:integration_tests/openai/test_handoffs.py:38-39,64-66; contrast AJ:…/AwsLiveTest.kt:51 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED (LS14 and LS17 merged in as its examples) |
| LT16 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Never retry a failed real-model assertion; retry only transport errors, and test the code's own retry by injecting the fault. | because blanket reruns hide flaky prompts | OA:integration_tests/openai/test_retry.py:34-44,78-79; contrast L4J:…/GlobalTestRetryExtension.java:23,37-38, SA:.github/actions/do-integration-test/action.yml:33-39 (research/69 §8) | | APPROVED |
| LS19 | testing/references/llm-live-tests.md | Give every live test an assertion that can fail, and check its gate does not always skip. | because a test that never runs or never asserts protects nothing | MA:…/prefill-error-recovery.e2e.test.ts:20,47; CA:e2e-tests/test_dynamic_control.py:44,79 (research/70 §6) | | APPROVED |

| SRC2 | meta/references/writing-references.md | Admit a rule only with two independent sources or the operator's stated preference, recorded in its ledger row, never in the reference. | because the reference is for the agent and provenance belongs in the ledger | operator 2026-10-31 (agent-factory#32 session): "This is generic skills nothing should be from agent platform"; "one company = 1 source"; "we need to add it in skills as this chiyanram skills not specific to agent platform that is my preference" | source audit of 157 code rules, 2026-10-31 (agent-factory#32) | AMENDED 2026-11-04 (operator yes, agent-factory#59): provenance lives in the ledger only; earlier text required a preference line and pair labels in the reference · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2) · Tool names stay when they are the action itself; a name that only credits a source moves to the ledger (operator, 2026-11-04) |
| SC23 | code/references/spring.md | Put `@Transactional(readOnly = true)` on a service class whose methods mostly read, and plain `@Transactional` on each write method. | because without the read-only default every query pays for dirty checking | plinth 311-spring-jdbc.md:665-668 (Example 10); Jeffallan spring-boot-engineer/references/data.md:153; Spring Data JPA SimpleJpaRepository §Transactionality (main ledger row SC23); earlier sources: see moved sources |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| CMN1 | code/references/comments.md | Delete commented-out code. | because it is never compiled, goes stale, and version control already keeps it | Sonar S125; clean-code-javascript; addy code-simplification:152; pstack comment-sicko:12 (research/48 §1 NEW-CM-a) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| CC11 | code/references/concurrency.md | Put a circuit breaker on a remote dependency whose failures repeat, beside the timeout and retry. | because retrying a dependency that is down wastes threads and amplifies the outage | ms-cb :29; ms-storm :43; pl123cc :22, :92-94 | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| DSN1 | code/references/design.md | Keep each method under the project's complexity gate (cyclomatic 10 if none is set), splitting by guard clauses or extracted helpers. | because a highly branched method hides paths no test reaches | Sonar S1541 (default 10, `MethodComplexityCheck`); addy code-simplification (guard clauses, helpers) (research/48 §1 NEW-DS-a) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| ERN1 | code/references/errors.md | Return 4xx for a client error and 500 with a generic body for an unforeseen fault; never a 2xx carrying an error. | because clients and monitoring read the status | OWASP-EH Error Handling Cheat Sheet (4xx client error, 5xx unforeseen bug) · ADDY api (500, never expose internals) · RFC9457 (same status code as the response) — r48 §2 2a new rule 1 | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| ERN2 | code/references/errors.md | Leave state as it was when a method fails: validate and compute before you mutate. | because a half-applied change corrupts data | EJ §64 (failure atomicity) · NET (no side effects when an exception is thrown) — r48 §2 2a new rule 2 | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| JVN1 | code/references/java.md | Compare `BigDecimal` values with `compareTo`, never `equals`. | because `equals` treats 2.0 and 2.00 as different | Sonar S9351; Error Prone `BigDecimalEquals` (research/48 §1 NEW-JV-a) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| MSN1 | code/references/messaging.md | Acknowledge or commit the offset only after the state change has committed. | because acking first loses the message if the consumer crashes in between | KAFKA docs/design/design.md (at-most-once); SBS idempotency-patterns (acknowledge only after commit) (r48 §3 :796) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| MSN2 | code/references/messaging.md | When a database change must also publish an event, write the event to an outbox in the same transaction and publish from there. | because publishing beside the write loses or invents events on a crash | PL123 Ex 2 (outbox); SBS event-driven-messaging (transactional outbox); DDH README (r48 §3 :797) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| SEN2 | code/references/security.md | Read every key and credential from the environment or a secret store, never a literal in code or config. | because committed secrets survive in history | OWASP Secrets Management CS; spring-boot-skills spring-security-jwt; addyosmani SKILL:89 (r48 §4.3 item 3) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| SEN1 | code/references/security.md | Rate-limit failed authentication attempts per account. | because an unthrottled login or key check lets an attacker guess passwords online | NIST 800-63B rate limiting; OWASP Authentication CS; addyosmani security-checklist:41 (r48 §4.3 item 1) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| LYN1 | code/references/services.md | Return response DTOs from a controller, never persistence entities. | because an entity in a response leaks every field it owns and ties the API to the schema | SBS layered-architecture (No JPA entities in responses); DDH README (DTO contract, data leaks) (r48 §3 :778) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| SCN1 | code/references/spring.md | Never inject a prototype-, request- or session-scoped bean straight into a singleton; inject `ObjectProvider<T>` and resolve it per use. | because the singleton keeps the first instance forever | FW factory-scopes; SONAR S6832 (r48 §3 :755) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |
| TYN1 | code/references/types.md | Never return `null` from a method whose type is `Optional`, and never compare an `Optional` to `null`. | because a null `Optional` throws `NullPointerException` where the caller expected empty | OpenJDK `Optional` apiNote; Sonar S2789; Error Prone `NullOptional` (research/48 §1 NEW-TY-a) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (re-sourced from industry, agent-factory#59) |

## Modern Java (agent-factory#60) — operator 2026-11-04: both batches approved; target the newest JDK the team runs (JDK 27)

Sources: agent-factory `research/49-modern-java.md` (r49). A project on an older JDK writes the form its JDK offers (a decided edge in modern-java.md).

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| MJ1 | code/references/modern-java.md | Declare an immutable data carrier as a record, never a class with hand-written accessors, `equals` and `hashCode`. | because a field added later is easily left out of `equals` and `hashCode` | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ1: OpenJDK amber "Data Classes and Sealed Types" + `java.lang.Record` javadoc @jdk-25-ga; HyperSkills java-spring `java-language.md`; plinth 144 Ex2; Jeffallan java-architect. Decided edge (JPA entity): HyperSkills | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ2 | code/references/modern-java.md | In a record or class holding a collection, store `List.copyOf`/`Set.copyOf`/`Map.copyOf` of the argument, never the argument, a view or an array. | because the caller keeps the original and can change the object later | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ2 + §2 MJ2 merged (§0): `java.lang.Record` javadoc @jdk-25-ga; `List` javadoc (`copyOf` unmodifiable); Error Prone `ArrayRecordComponent`; Effective Java summary Item 50; plinth 128, 123, 142 Ex1. Decided edge (null element): `List` javadoc, TY12 | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ3 | code/references/modern-java.md | Test and bind in one step with `x instanceof Type t`; never follow an `instanceof` test with a cast. | because the repeated type name can drift from the test and the cast then throws `ClassCastException` | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ3: OpenJDK amber "Pattern Matching for Java"; Error Prone `PatternMatchingInstanceof`; plinth 142 Ex9 | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ4 | code/references/modern-java.md | Dispatch on a sealed type's subtypes with a pattern `switch`, never an `if`/`else if` chain of `instanceof` tests. | because the chain has no exhaustiveness check, so a new subtype silently takes the last branch | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ4: OpenJDK amber "Pattern Matching for Java" + exhaustiveness guide; Error Prone `IfChainToSwitch`; HyperSkills java-spring | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ5 | code/references/modern-java.md | Write every `switch` arrow-form (`case X ->`), and make one that yields a value a switch expression. | because a colon-form case without `break` falls through and a statement case can forget to assign | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ5: Error Prone `StatementSwitchToExpressionSwitch` + Google Java Style 4.8.4 (one org); OpenJDK amber "Type Patterns in switch" + "Pattern Matching for Java"; plinth 142. Decided edge (`default` on int/String): amber exhaustiveness guide | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ6 | code/references/modern-java.md | Name an intentionally unused catch parameter, lambda parameter or pattern variable `_`. | because a named unused variable reads as used and hides a forgotten use from the unused-variable check | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ6: Google Java Style 5.2.9/6.2 + Error Prone `UnnamedVariable` (one org); HyperSkills java-spring | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ8 | code/references/modern-java.md | Write production code as named classes in a package; keep compact source files (no class header) to scripts outside `src/main`. | because a compact source file cannot declare a package, so no class can import it | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ8: javac `compiler.properties` @jdk-25-ga + amber "Paving the On-Ramp" (OpenJDK, fact); HyperSkills java-spring; gleicon devskills ds-java-review 25.md | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ9 | code/references/modern-java.md | Never compile or run production code with `--enable-preview`; use only features final in the target JDK. | because a preview feature may change or vanish next release, and its class files load only on that JDK | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §1 MJ9: javac `compiler.properties` preview warnings @jdk-25-ga; HyperSkills java-spring; gleicon devskills ds-java-review 25.md | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ10 | code/references/modern-java.md | Collect a stream with `toList()` when no one mutates the result, and with `Collectors.toCollection(ArrayList::new)` when the caller must; never mutate a `Collectors.toList()` result. | because `toList()` throws on mutation and `Collectors.toList()` promises no mutability | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §2 MJ1: `Stream.toList` + `Collectors.toList` javadoc @jdk-25-ga; Error Prone `MixedMutabilityReturnType`; plinth 142 Ex2. Decided edge (null element): `Collectors` javadoc | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ12 | code/references/modern-java.md | Unwrap an `Optional` with `map`, `orElse`, `orElseGet` or `orElseThrow`; never call `get()`, and never test `isPresent()` then `get()`. | because `get()` hides that it throws `NoSuchElementException` when empty | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §2 MJ3: `Optional.get` javadoc @jdk-25-ga; plinth 142 Ex14, 141; Error Prone `OptionalNotPresent` (supporting) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ13 | code/references/modern-java.md | Add, multiply or narrow counts and minor-unit money with `Math.addExact`, `multiplyExact` and `toIntExact`, never bare `+`, `*` or an `(int)` cast. | because `int` and `long` overflow wraps silently to a wrong value | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §2 MJ4: `Math` class javadoc @jdk-25-ga (`*Exact`); Error Prone `IntLongMath` (failure only) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ14 | code/references/modern-java.md | Use `ArrayList`, `HashMap`, `ArrayDeque` and `StringBuilder`, never `Vector`, `Hashtable`, `Stack` or `StringBuffer`; share state through a `java.util.concurrent` type. | because the legacy classes synchronize every call and pay for locking nobody needs | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §2 MJ5: `Vector`, `Hashtable`, `Stack`, `StringBuffer` javadoc @jdk-25-ga; Error Prone `JdkObsolete` | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ15 | code/references/modern-java.md | Box a primitive by autoboxing or `valueOf`, never `new Integer(..)`, `new Long(..)` or another wrapper constructor. | because each constructor call allocates a fresh object that `==` and the value caches treat as distinct | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §2 MJ6: `Integer` constructor javadoc @jdk-25-ga; Error Prone `BoxedPrimitiveConstructor`; Checkstyle `IllegalInstantiation` | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ16 | code/references/modern-java.md | Never override `finalize()`; release a resource through `close()` under try-with-resources, or register a `Cleaner` as a safety net. | because finalization is deprecated for removal and may run late or never | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §2 MJ7: `Object.finalize` javadoc @jdk-25-ga; Error Prone `Finalize` (Google Java Style 6.4); Checkstyle `NoFinalizer`; Effective Java summary Item 8 | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60) |
| MJ17 | code/references/modern-java.md | Set the Java level with `--release` (Maven `maven.compiler.release` or `<release>`), never `-source`/`-target` or `maven.compiler.source`/`target`. | because those compile against the build JDK's API and can emit classes that fail on the target JDK | r49 = agent-factory research/49-modern-java.md: §2 MJ8: javac warning text in `compiler.properties` + javac manual `--release` @jdk-25-ga (vendor guidance); plinth 112 (supporting) | | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (agent-factory#60), vendor docs (decision V) |

SR8 merges the design-review rubric into spec-review.md. Candidates SR9–SR16 were read against seven reference
projects on 2026-11-04 (pstack `bb68f236`, mattpocock `4ae2317d`, addyosmani `301b02b1`, plinth `45ead2d8`, Jeffallan
`078a25cc`, firstmate `bb8dd209`, humanlayer `77c7fe99`); each has two independent sources. SR16 is merged into SR4.
The verdict words, output format and stage wiring of the reviewer stay with agent-factory T10 (#40). The Good/Bad pairs
for SR10 and SR11 are fresh neutral code.

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SR4 | planning/references/spec-review.md | Before approval, look up what code and docs answer, then give each open question left a decider, options with a recommendation, and why it does not block building. | because a bare question outlives the build | Supersedes the SR4 row above. Decider half: as that row; options and recommendation (was candidate SR16): mattpocock `skills/productivity/grilling/SKILL.md:8,28` @4ae2317d ("give your recommended answer"; "Finding _facts_ is your job, never the user's": the look-up-first half); kunchenguid/firstmate `.agents/skills/ask-user-authority/SKILL.md:39-45` @bb8dd209 (element 5: "A recommendation with the reason"); pstack `pstack/skills/architect/references/rationale-template.md:31` @bb68f236 agrees |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (SR8: amended, SR16 merged in) |
| SR9 | planning/references/spec-review.md | Check each design decision against the ADRs and patterns it touches; report an unnamed departure, or a conflict between documents, without resolving it. | because a silent departure overrides a recorded decision | mattpocock `skills/engineering/to-spec/SKILL.md:13` @4ae2317d ("respect any ADRs in the area you're touching"); addyosmani `skills/code-review-and-quality/SKILL.md:55` @301b02b1 ("follow existing patterns or introduce a new one? If new, is it justified?"); conflict half: jabrena/plinth `skills/042-planning-openspec/references/042-planning-openspec.md:128` @45ead2d8 ("Report the conflict"); firstmate `ask-user-authority/SKILL.md:24` @bb8dd209 ("Reviewer language cannot amend that contract"). Overlaps SR5 (HOLD), which stays as it is |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (SR8) |

Edge (forced shape): rationale-template:27 ("the only viable shape because"); mattpocock `domain-modeling/ADR-FORMAT.md:22` |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (SR8) |
| SR11 | planning/references/spec-review.md | For each step that changes state or calls another system, say in the design what happens when it fails, runs twice, or crashes halfway. | because partial state left behind turns every retry into debugging | pstack `pstack/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent/SKILL.md:9` @bb68f236 ("What happens if this runs twice? What happens if the previous run crashed halfway?"); Jeffallan `skills/architecture-designer/SKILL.md:60` @078a25cc ("Plan for failure modes"); plinth `123-cross-cutting-integration-patterns.md:206` @45ead2d8 agrees |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (SR8) |
| SR12 | planning/references/spec-review.md | When the design changes stored data, a public contract or a deploy, name the migration and the rollback, and mark each irreversible step for the human's yes. | because a shipped irreversible step cannot be undone | jabrena/plinth `skills/056-design-avoid-breaking-changes/references/056-design-avoid-breaking-changes.md:32` @45ead2d8 ("deprecation, compatibility-window, migration, validation"); addyosmani `skills/doubt-driven-development/SKILL.md:22` @301b02b1 ("blast radius is irreversible (production deploy, data migration, public API change)"); firstmate `ask-user-authority/SKILL.md:34` @bb8dd209 agrees; humanlayer `create_plan.md:329` @77c7fe99 ("Consider migration and rollback") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-04 (SR8) |

## Moved sources

On 2026-11-04 the operator decided that a reference holds only what an agent needs to act (agent-factory#59). The strip removed citations from every reference. Most were already in each rule's row above. The ones below were not, so they are kept here, as they stood in the references at `52ae5e8`. ### Code and deslop

Every line of the code references and `deslop/SKILL.md` on main at `52ae5e8` that carried a source, a citation, a
credit, a sources paragraph or an operator-preference mark, with its line number in that file. The T17 code re-check
(agent-factory research/48) then reworded or replaced some of these rules; the ledger rows hold the current sources,
and this section keeps what the old text cited.

### code/references/angular-ui.md

- :45 | a value whose type the code cannot know | `unknown`, then narrow it (NGA2) | `any` switches off the check on every property access · angular.dev best practices "use `unknown` when type is uncertain"; Google TypeScript Style Guide §Using unknown over any; pstack typescript-best-practices "External data is `unknown`" |
- :46 | an untyped library that forces `any` | a `no-explicit-any` suppression with a one-line reason naming the library (NGA2) | Google TypeScript Style Guide §Suppressing any lint warnings "document why it is legitimate" |
- :47 | a value that must match a type | `satisfies`, not `as` (NGA6) | `as` hides a crash the compiler could catch (NGA3) · TypeScript 4.9 release notes §satisfies; pstack typescript-best-practices "`satisfies` over `as`"; Jeffallan typescript-pro |
- :48 | a `switch` over a union | `const _exhaustive: never = x` in the default arm (NGA5) | a new variant fails to compile · TypeScript handbook §Exhaustiveness checking; pstack typescript-best-practices; Jeffallan typescript-pro |
- :49 | a frontend type for a backend DTO | a type derived from the OpenAPI schema (NGA7) | a hand-written copy drifts · operator preference; pstack `references/patterns.md` "derive from the generated types instead of duplicating" |
- :52 | a dependency in a component or service | `inject()` in a field initialiser (NGC1) | operator preference · angular.dev style guide §Prefer the inject function over constructor parameter injection |
- :55 | a list in a template | `@for` with `track` (NGD1) | `track` maps items to DOM nodes and speeds rendering · angular.dev control flow §Why is track in @for blocks important; Jeffallan angular-architect `references/components.md` |
- :59 Operator preference, 2026-10-31 (SRC2), in whole or in part: NGA7 and NGC1.

- :81 **Good** (neutral)
- :102 reason that names the untyped library (NGA2; Google TypeScript Style Guide §Suppressing any lint warnings).

### code/references/comments.md

- :29 | a banner or divider | delete it (CM1) | banners are on the kill list (sicko :12) |
- :30 | narration of what the code, a dependency or a config value does | delete it (CM2) | narration is on the kill list (sicko :12); a comment that explains "what" goes (simplification :144) |
- :31 | a legal or license header | keep it (CM3) | sicko :16 |
- :32 | behaviour forced by an external dependency, platform, vendor or protocol | keep it (CM3) | sicko :17 |
- :33 | a surprise in our own code | delete it; rename, extract or type the symbol until the behaviour is obvious (CM3) | sicko :17 |
- :34 | a doc comment that defines a public API contract | keep it when the signature does not show the contract (CM3, CM5) | sicko :19; an interface is also its invariants, ordering constraints and error modes (codebase-design :16) |
- :35 | an issue or RFC link for a constraint the code cannot express | keep it (CM3) | sicko :20 |
- :36 | a constraint note: `do not remove`, `do not change wording`, `talk to X before changing` | check the claim; encode it as a type, test or lint, then delete it (CM4) | sicko :26; no-comments :23 |
- :37 | a lint or compiler suppression | fix the type when the rule protects correctness or safety; keep it when the rule is style-only (CM7) | sicko :18, :24 |
- :39 `sicko` is pstack `agents/comment-sicko.md`, `no-comments` is pstack `skills/no-comments/SKILL.md`, `simplification`
- :40 is addy `skills/code-simplification/SKILL.md` and `codebase-design` is mattpocock
- :41 `skills/engineering/codebase-design/SKILL.md`; `:N` is the line.
- :43 Operator preference, 2026-10-31 (SRC2), in whole or in part: CM4 and CM5.

- :85 **Good** (neutral)

- :111 **Good** (neutral)

- :133 skip when** you are not sure a keep clause applies: the comment goes (sicko :22). — **Confidence** high
- :135 code, even though addy keeps such comments (simplification :145): delete it and ask for a reshape (operator
- :136 preference, 2026-10-31). — **Confidence** high
- :137 - **Skip when** a member's contract is obvious from its signature: write no Javadoc (Google Java Style §7.3.1 makes it
- :138 optional for a simple, obvious member, and §7.3.2 for an override). — **Do not skip when** the interface has an
- :139 invariant, ordering constraint or error mode the signature cannot show (codebase-design :16). — **Confidence** high

### code/references/concurrency.md

- :40 | many blocking I/O calls to run at once | `Executors.newVirtualThreadPerTaskExecutor()`, one virtual thread per task, never a pool of them (CC1) | avoid pooling virtual threads (pl125 :31) · there is never a need to pool them (JEP 444 §Do not pool virtual threads). The `synchronized` and pinning clauses are operator preference, read in JEP 491 (§Future Work, §Diagnosing remaining cases of pinning) |
- :41 | blocking work to run async | `supplyAsync(task, executor)` with a dedicated executor (CC2) | operator preference; avoid blocking I/O in the common pool; use a dedicated executor (pl125 :37) · the two-argument form (pl142 :854) |
- :42 | CPU-bound, short, side-effect-free work | ForkJoin or a parallel stream, outside request- or queue-serving code (CC2; JV2, `java.md`) | the common pool is reserved for that work (pl125 :37) |
- :43 | a client for a remote service | a connect and a read timeout on the client itself (CC3) | always bound remote calls with timeouts (pl125 :40) · cancel ongoing work on timeout (pl126 :34) · set a timeout on any remote call (AWS timeouts) |
- :44 | a remote call with a side effect | an intent record before the call, and three outcomes: success, failure, unknown (CC9) | operator preference; a timeout tells you nothing about whether the effect applied (addy api :213) |
- :45 | an idempotency key | one key per intent, minted by the initiator and reused on every retry (CC9) | never from the layer doing the retrying (addy api :163, :171) · the client generates the idempotency key (Stripe idempotent requests) |
- :46 | a failed remote call to retry | the timeout first, then bounded attempts with jitter, for transient failures of idempotent calls only (CC8) | bounded attempts and jitter (pl123cc :42-43) · retry only transient failures (pl123cc :95) · limit retries, add jitter, retry side effects only when idempotent (AWS timeouts) |
- :47 | a dependency that can exhaust shared threads, connections or queue capacity | a bulkhead: a bounded pool, a bounded queue and a rejection policy (CC4) | bounded queues and rejection policies (pl125 :35) · an unbounded work queue can grow without limit (JDK `ThreadPoolExecutor`) · a bounded queue and a fixed thread pool (Resilience4j bulkhead) |
- :48 | two actors that write the same state | each actor its own key or file, merged where it is read (CC5) | operator preference; eliminate the shared write target first (ps separate :15) |
- :49 | two locks held at once | one global acquisition order (CC5) | operator preference; establish global lock ordering (pl125 :36) |
- :50 | a `CompletableFuture` pipeline | `thenApply`/`thenCompose`/`allOf`; block only at the outer boundary (CC6) | operator preference; avoid premature `join()`/`get()` mid-flow (pl142 :839) |
- :51 | context that async tasks read | a `ScopedValue` (CC7) | `ScopedValue` over `ThreadLocal` across tasks (pl125 :33, :39) · scoped values may be a better alternative (JEP 444 §Thread-local variables; JEP 487 §Expensive inheritance) |
- :52 | a cache | an eviction policy and a memory ceiling (CC10) | eviction policy and memory ceiling set (addy perf :181) · size-based eviction with `maximumSize` (Caffeine Eviction §Size-based) |
- :54 Sources, all read at a pinned commit or at the URL named; `:N` is a line:
- :56 - pl125, pl126, pl142: jabrena/plinth `skills/<n>-<name>/references/<n>-<name>.md` for 125-java-concurrency,
- :57 126-java-exception-handling and 142-java-functional-programming, @a46375a.
- :58 - pl123cc: jabrena/plinth `skills/123-java-design-patterns/references/123-cross-cutting-integration-patterns.md`,
- :59 @a46375a.
- :60 - addy api, addy perf: addyosmani/agent-skills `skills/api-and-interface-design/SKILL.md` and
- :61 `references/performance-checklist.md`, @aeac41d.
- :62 - ps separate: pstack `skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md`, @f713f14.
- :63 - JEP 444, JEP 487, JEP 491: OpenJDK, https://openjdk.org/jeps/444, /487 and /491 (§Future Work, §Diagnosing remaining
- :64 cases of pinning).
- :65 - AWS timeouts: Amazon Builders' Library, "Timeouts, retries, and backoff with jitter",
- :66 https://aws.amazon.com/builders-library/timeouts-retries-and-backoff-with-jitter/.
- :67 - JDK `ThreadPoolExecutor`: Java SE 25 Javadoc, `java.util.concurrent.ThreadPoolExecutor` §Queuing, §Rejected tasks.
- :68 - Resilience4j bulkhead: https://resilience4j.readme.io/docs/bulkhead.
- :69 - Stripe idempotent requests: https://docs.stripe.com/api/idempotent_requests.
- :70 - Caffeine Eviction: https://github.com/ben-manes/caffeine/wiki/Eviction §Size-based.

### code/references/design.md

- :15 | more than one job | one seam per job (SEAM1) | a class has one job, one reason to change (pl121p Example 2); operator preference |
- :16 | an input | one record (SEAM1) | operator preference |
- :17 | an outcome to return | a sealed result (SEAM1); its caller switches over it with no `default` arm (TY2, `types.md`) | the compiler must fail when a new variant is not handled (pstack :20); the compiler confirms every permitted subclass is covered (JEP 409) |
- :18 | a decision to make | a pure function that calls no I/O (SEAM1) | isolate side effects at the edges (pl142 :740); return results, do not produce side effects (mattpocock `codebase-design/SKILL.md` :83) |
- :19 | I/O to do | a method that does the I/O and chooses no outcome (SEAM1) | isolate side effects at the edges (pl142 :740) |
- :20 | a choice among several cases | a rule list, not an if-ladder; each method at cyclomatic complexity ≤5 (SEAM1) | operator preference |
- :21 | a value that may be absent | `Optional`, a sealed type or an enum (TY1, `types.md`) | a bag of optional fields lets contradictory combinations compile (pstack :15) |
- :24 `pstack` is cursor-plugins pstack `skills/principle-type-system-discipline/SKILL.md`. `pl121p` is plinth
- :25 `121-java-object-oriented-design-principles.md` and `pl142` is plinth `142-java-functional-programming.md`. `:N` is
- :26 the line. The sealed result and the pure decision are industry rules; the other SEAM1 parts are operator preference.
- :28 Operator preference, 2026-10-31 (SRC2), in whole or in part: SEAM1.

### code/references/errors.md

- :39 | a case the caller can foresee: not found, already exists, over limit | a sealed outcome in the return type (ER1), never an `Either` (ER7) | operator preference; a case the types let you ignore becomes a runtime failure the compiler could have stopped (types :9) |
- :40 | a fault: a non-success the caller cannot foresee | throw the one base exception type with a machine-readable error code (ER2) | operator preference; every error response follows one shape with a machine-readable code (api :61-86) |
- :41 | an exception that must become an HTTP response | the one central exception handler (ER11), with the error code's generic message as the body (ER10) | translate at the boundary with centralized handlers (exc :32, :2184) · define a global error handler (OWASP) · never expose stack traces or internal error details (sec :73, :98) · log the detail, give users a generic message (exc :26) |
- :42 | a caught exception that is rethrown | the caught exception passed as the cause (ER3) | operator preference; chaining keeps the full error context (exc :27) |
- :43 | a caught exception that is not rethrown | handle it or log it, never an empty block (ER3) | an empty catch block hides the problem (exc :1948) · errors silently swallowed (rubric :10) |
- :44 | an `InterruptedException` | `Thread.currentThread().interrupt()`, or a rethrow (ER5) | operator preference; restore the interrupted status (exc :28) |
- :45 | a `catch` clause | the narrowest type the `try` can raise (ER4) | operator preference for the narrowest type and the must-not-fail boundary; specific types, not generic `Exception` (exc :23) · `Throwable` or `Error` can mask `OutOfMemoryError` (exc :2374); an `Error` is a problem a reasonable application should not try to catch (JDK `Error`) |
- :46 | a best-effort metric or audit call that fails | catch, log at WARN with the exception (LG3, `../../obs/references/logging.md`), continue (ER8) | WARN means degraded but handled (obs :75) · an empty catch block hides the problem (exc :1948) |
- :47 | request input | Bean Validation at the boundary, no second check inside (ER6) | validate at the boundary, trust the types inside (boundary :14-15, :24) · validate at system edges (api :90) · Bean Validation of web input (Spring Framework, Validation) |

### code/references/smells.md

- :67 | a name that does not say what it holds or does | possible **Mysterious Name**: rename it (NM1, NM2) | mp :45 |
- :68 | the same logic shape in two hunks or files | possible **Duplicated Code**: extract it when it is a rule (DS12), not otherwise (DS14) | mp :46 |
- :69 | a method that reads another object's data more than its own | possible **Feature Envy**: move the method to that data; for a record, FW6 | mp :47 |
- :70 | the same fields or parameters travelling together | possible **Data Clumps**: bundle them into one type | mp :48 |
- :71 | a primitive or string standing for a domain concept | possible **Primitive Obsession**: give it its own small type (TY8, `types.md`) | mp :49 |
- :72 | the same `switch` or `if` cascade on one type at several sites | possible **Repeated Switches**: one sealed type or one shared map (DS3) | mp :50 |
- :73 | one logical change that edits many scattered files | possible **Shotgun Surgery**: gather what changes together into one module | mp :51 |
- :74 | one module edited for several unrelated reasons | possible **Divergent Change**: split it so each part changes for one reason | mp :52 |
- :75 | a parameter, hook or abstraction that no current need uses | possible **Speculative Generality**: delete it (DS6) | mp :53 |
- :76 | a caller walking `a.b().c().d()` | possible **Message Chains**: hide the walk behind one method (FW8) | mp :54 |
- :77 | a class or method that mostly delegates | possible **Middle Man**: call the real target (DS4, FW8) | mp :55 |
- :78 | a subclass or implementer that ignores most of what it inherits | possible **Refused Bequest**: drop the inheritance and use composition | mp :56 |
- :87 | a class doing too much: many fields, many responsibilities | possible **Large Class**: Extract Class; a file over 1000 lines is the DS2 operator preference | F 20 |
- :91 | nested or compound conditionals | possible **Conditional Complexity**: model the cases (DS3); nesting is DS9 (`../../deslop/SKILL.md`) | operator preference (SM2) |
- :92 | one problem solved two different ways in one code base | possible **Oddball Solution**: keep one way (NM4) | operator preference (SM2) |
- :93 | a class or method visible beyond the code that needs it | possible **Indecent Exposure**: narrow its visibility (SX14, `spring.md`) | operator preference (SM2) |
- :94 | an unused private member or an unreachable branch | possible **Dead Code**: delete it (DS7, `../../deslop/SKILL.md`) | SM3 · guru dead-code · simplification :152 |
- :96 `mp` is mattpocock `skills/engineering/code-review/SKILL.md` (plugin 1.2.3). `F n` is _Refactoring_ 2e ch. 3 at
- :97 InformIT, `seqNum=n`. `guru` is
- :98 refactoring.guru and `simplification` is addy
- :99 `skills/code-simplification/SKILL.md`. `:N` is the line.
- :100 Smell names checked against refactoring.com; the 2e smell-to-cure pairs were not checked against the book text.
- :102 Operator preference, 2026-10-31 (SRC2), in whole or in part: DS11, SM2, DS1, DS14, DS2, FW1, NM2 and NM4.

- :45 | a value that may be absent | `Optional` as a return type (JV6, `java.md`), a sealed interface with records, or an enum (TY1) | a bag of optional fields lets contradictory combinations compile (pstack :15); `Optional` states absence explicitly (pl141 :206) |
- :46 | a closed set of two or more cases | a sealed interface or an enum, and a switch with no `default` arm (TY2) | the compiler must fail when a new variant is not handled (pstack :20, :29); the compiler confirms every permitted subclass is covered (JEP 409) |
- :47 | a record whose fields are legal only in some combinations | one record per legal shape under a sealed interface; the compact constructor for what the shapes cannot exclude (TY3) | a comment that explains when fields are valid means the type is too loose (pstack :15, :26); sealed classes restrict the subclasses to model the domain (JEP 409); the compact-constructor clause is operator preference |
- :48 | two parameters of one primitive type that mean different things | one wrapping record per meaning (TY8) | same-typed arguments that mean different things are branded (pstack :17, :27); give the concept its own small type (mattpocock `code-review/SKILL.md` :49); type-safe wrappers (pl122 Example 4) |

- :54 | an unchecked warning | the generic type fixed; else `@SuppressWarnings("unchecked")` on one statement, with its reason (TY10); any other suppression is CM7, `comments.md` | raw types defer generic type checks to runtime (Java Tutorial *Raw Types*); suppress only provably safe operations, as narrowly as possible (pl128 :1504-1506) |
- :55 | a value to construct | its record constructor or a static factory (TY13) | prefer constructors, static factories and records first (pl123 :22; pl121c Example 2) |

## Persistence (#57)

PS1–PS14 for `code/references/persistence.md`, from the light research for #57 (`57-persistence.md`). Hibernate pages count as one source; Spring Boot, Spring Data JPA and Spring Framework as one. Operator decisions 2026-11-05: (1) PS11 only for entities inserted in bulk, with a one-line cross-reference to MG36 in both files; (2) one Decided edge exempts a JPA entity's persistent fields and pre-persist id from TY4, MJ1 and TY1; (3) PS13 owns the `@Version` mapping only; (4) the integration-tests example stops setting `minimum-idle`; (5) no rule for remote calls inside a transaction.

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PS1 | code/references/persistence.md | Declare every `@ManyToOne` and `@OneToOne` `fetch = FetchType.LAZY`, and never write `FetchType.EAGER`. | because JPA's eager to-one default loads the associated row on every query, often as one extra select per result | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/introduction/Entities.adoc` ("Almost all associations should be lazy."; "**never** write `@ManyToMany(fetch=EAGER)`"); vladmihalcea/hypersistence-optimizer@master `hypersistence-optimizer-spring-boot3-hibernate7-example/src/test/java/io/hypersistence/optimizer/forum/ApplicationTest.java` (`EagerFetchingEvent`); jabrena/plinth `skills/123-java-design-patterns/references/123-database-persistence-patterns.md` ("Watch for … accidental eager loading"); Jeffallan/claude-skills `skills/java-architect/references/jpa-optimization.md` (`@ManyToOne(fetch = FetchType.LAZY)`) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS2 | code/references/persistence.md | Fetch each association a use case navigates in the owner's query (`join fetch`, `@EntityGraph` or a DTO projection), never lazily per result element. | because each element then costs one more select (N+1) | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/introduction/Tuning.adoc` ("the most common cause of poorly-performing data access code"; "you should almost always use outer join fetching"); jabrena/plinth `skills/123-java-design-patterns/references/123-database-persistence-patterns.md` ("Watch for N+1 queries"); Jeffallan/claude-skills `skills/java-architect/references/jpa-optimization.md` ("N+1 problem solved with JOIN FETCH") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS3 | code/references/persistence.md | Fetch at most one collection per query; load each further collection with its own query or `@BatchSize`. | because two collection fetches multiply rows into a Cartesian product or throw `MultipleBagFetchException` | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/fetching/Fetching.adoc` ("Fetching multiple bags in the same plan may raise `MultipleBagFetchException`"); hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/introduction/Tuning.adoc` ("would result in a cartesian product of tables"). Hibernate alone (its own exception). Counter-source: Jeffallan/claude-skills `skills/java-architect/references/jpa-optimization.md` (`attributePaths = {"addresses", "roles"}`) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (vendor docs, decision V, #57) |
| PS4 | code/references/persistence.md | Never page (`Pageable`, `Limit`, `setMaxResults`) a query that fetches a collection; page the owner ids, then fetch the collection for those ids. | because Hibernate loads every matching row and pages in memory | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/fetching/Fetching.adoc` ("Hibernate may fetch all matching rows and apply the limit in memory."; names `hibernate.query.fail_on_pagination_over_collection_fetch`); vladmihalcea/hypersistence-optimizer@master `hypersistence-optimizer-spring-boot3-hibernate7-example/src/test/java/io/hypersistence/optimizer/forum/ApplicationTest.java` (`QueryPaginationCollectionFetchingEvent`) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS5 | code/references/persistence.md | Give every paged or limited query an `ORDER BY` that ends on a unique column, such as the id. | because without a unique order PostgreSQL returns an unpredictable subset and pages skip or repeat rows | postgres/postgres@master `doc/src/sgml/queries.sgml` LIMIT and OFFSET ("Otherwise you will get an unpredictable subset of the query's rows."); vladmihalcea/hypersistence-optimizer@master `hypersistence-optimizer-spring-boot3-hibernate7-example/src/test/java/io/hypersistence/optimizer/forum/ApplicationTest.java` (`PaginationWithoutOrderByEvent`). Query correctness only; the API page shape is #74 |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS6 | code/references/persistence.md | Bound every query whose result grows with the data with a `Pageable`, `Limit` or SQL `LIMIT`; never return the whole table as a `List` (`findAll()`). | because each call then loads it into the heap | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/appendices/BestPractices.adoc` ("Use bounded result sets where the operation permits them."); jabrena/plinth `skills/123-java-design-patterns/references/123-database-persistence-patterns.md` ("Watch for … unbounded result sets"). Query bounding only; API pagination is #74 |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS7 | code/references/persistence.md | Set `spring.jpa.open-in-view: false`. | because Boot's default keeps the persistence context open through response rendering, so lazy loads there run unplanned queries outside the service's transaction | spring-projects/spring-boot@main `module/spring-boot-jpa/src/main/java/org/springframework/boot/jpa/autoconfigure/JpaBaseConfiguration.java` ("database queries may be performed during view rendering"); spring-boot@main `documentation/spring-boot-docs/src/docs/antora/modules/reference/pages/data/sql.adoc` ("set `spring.jpa.open-in-view` to `false`"); hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/appendices/BestPractices.adoc` ("Load the state needed outside the persistence context before … closing"); Jeffallan/claude-skills `skills/java-architect/references/spring-boot-setup.md` (`open-in-view: false`) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS8 | code/references/persistence.md | Write `@Modifying(flushAutomatically = true, clearAutomatically = true)` on every modifying repository query. | because the bulk statement bypasses the persistence context: it misses pending changes and leaves managed entities stale | spring-projects/spring-data-jpa@main `src/main/antora/modules/ROOT/pages/jpa/query-methods.adoc` ("`EntityManager` might contain outdated entities after the execution"), `spring-data-jpa/src/main/java/org/springframework/data/jpa/repository/Modifying.java` ("flush the underlying persistence context before executing"); hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/caching/Caching.adoc` ("does not update objects already managed in the persistence context") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS9 | code/references/persistence.md | Change rows that all get the same new value with one `@Modifying` update or delete, never by loading entities and setting fields in a loop. | because dirty checking loads every row, one `UPDATE` each | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/introduction/Tuning.adoc` ("Even better than DML statement batching is the use of HQL `update`"); hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/batch/Batching.adoc` ("Set-based changes without loading each matching entity"); Jeffallan/claude-skills `skills/spring-boot-engineer/references/data.md` ("`@Modifying` \| Bulk updates/deletes", weak) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS10 | code/references/persistence.md | Set `spring.jpa.properties.hibernate.jdbc.batch_size`, starting between 10 and 50. | because Hibernate's default of 0 sends every insert and update in its own round trip | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/batch/Batching.adoc` ("Its default is zero; zero or a negative value disables batching."); hibernate-orm@6.6 same path ("an integer between 10 and 50"); spring-projects/spring-boot@main `documentation/spring-boot-docs/src/docs/antora/modules/how-to/pages/data-access.adoc` ("you must use `spring.jpa.properties.hibernate.jdbc.batch_size`"); Jeffallan/claude-skills `skills/java-architect/references/spring-boot-setup.md` (`batch_size: 20`) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS11 | code/references/persistence.md | For an entity inserted in bulk, map its generated id as `GenerationType.SEQUENCE` with a `@SequenceGenerator` whose `allocationSize` equals the sequence's `INCREMENT BY`, never `IDENTITY`. | because Hibernate cannot batch inserts of `IDENTITY` entities | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/domain/identifiers.adoc` ("Hibernate will not be able to batch INSERT statements"); hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/appendices/BestPractices.adoc` ("Keep the mapping's allocation size … and database sequence increment compatible"); vladmihalcea/hypersistence-optimizer@master `hypersistence-optimizer-spring-boot3-hibernate7-example/src/test/java/io/hypersistence/optimizer/forum/ApplicationTest.java` (`IdentityGeneratorEvent`). Counter-source: Jeffallan/claude-skills (uses `IDENTITY`). Scoped to entities inserted in bulk (decision 1); cross-referenced with MG36, whose text is unchanged; the key column of a PS11 entity is `GENERATED BY DEFAULT AS IDENTITY (INCREMENT BY 50)`, because `ALWAYS` rejects assigned ids (PostgreSQL CREATE TABLE "GENERATED ALWAYS … unless OVERRIDING SYSTEM VALUE"; review round 1, operator yes 2026-11-06) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS12 | code/references/persistence.md | Base an entity's `equals` and `hashCode` on its immutable `@NaturalId`, using `instanceof` and accessor reads; never a generated id, a mutable field or `getClass()`. | because ids appear only on persist and proxies fail `getClass()` | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/introduction/Entities.adoc` ("advise against including any database-generated field in the hashcode"; "use `instanceof`, not `getClass()`"). Hibernate alone (its own proxy and id behaviour). Entity exemption from TY4, MJ1, TY1 is a Decided edge (decision 2) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (vendor docs, decision V, #57) |
| PS13 | code/references/persistence.md | Give every entity that two requests can update concurrently a `@Version` field, never set by hand. | because without it the later of two edits silently overwrites the earlier | hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/introduction/Tuning.adoc` ("For multiuser systems, optimistic locking is king."); hibernate/hibernate-orm@main `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/transactions/Transactions.adoc` ("Use optimistic version checks to detect conflicting writes"); jabrena/plinth `skills/123-java-design-patterns/references/123-database-persistence-patterns.md` ("stale writes fail instead of overwriting newer data"). Mapping only; conflict handling (409, retry, idempotency) is a later rule set (decision 3) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (#57) |
| PS14 | code/references/persistence.md | Run Hikari as a small fixed pool: `maximum-pool-size` from a load test, starting near twice the database's cores, a few dozen at most, no `minimum-idle`. | because surplus connections contend and lower throughput | brettwooldridge/HikariCP.wiki `About-Pool-Sizing.md` ("You want a small pool of a few dozen connections at most"; "connections = ((core_count * 2) + effective_spindle_count)"); brettwooldridge/HikariCP `README.md` ("we recommend *not* setting this value", `minimumIdle`). HikariCP alone (its own pool). CC10 already requires a maximum size and an acquire timeout. `testing/references/examples/integration-tests.md` SP7 no longer sets `minimum-idle` (decision 4) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-05 (vendor docs, decision V, #57) |

## Resilience (#54)

CC12, CC14, CC15 and CC16 for `code/references/concurrency.md`, from the light research for #54. Most of the ticket was already covered (CC3, CC8, CC9, CC11, SC22, SE8, `obs/references/levels.md`). Google SRE and gRPC count as one source. Operator decisions 2026-11-06: CC13 (a client-wide retry budget; Google SRE Handling Overload "per-client retry budget (a 10% retry ratio)", twitter/finagle@ca472de `doc/src/sphinx/Clients.rst:272` "approximately 20%") is not admitted, because neither Resilience4j nor Spring Retry ships one; 408 is dropped from CC16 because no source names it. Bulkheads (CC4, CC10 cover them), fallbacks and server-side load shedding are left out.

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC12 | code/references/concurrency.md | Retry a failure at one layer only; the layers below fail fast and report the cause. | because attempts multiply across layers: three layers of three attempts send 27 requests | Google SRE book, "Addressing Cascading Failures", Limit retries per request ("avoid amplifying retries by issuing retries at multiple levels: a single request at the highest layer may produce a number of attempts as large as the product of the number of attempts at each layer"); MicrosoftDocs/architecture-center@1256c44 `docs/patterns/retry-content.md:68` ("configure the lower-level task to fail fast and report the reason for the failure back to the task that invoked it") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#54) |
| CC14 | code/references/concurrency.md | Pass the caller's remaining deadline to each downstream call and stop once it has passed, never a fixed longer timeout. | because work for a caller who gave up wastes the capacity recovery needs | Google SRE book, "Addressing Cascading Failures", Deadline propagation ("Rather than inventing a deadline when sending RPCs to backends, servers should employ deadline propagation"); grpc/grpc.io@d4b8085 `content/en/docs/guides/deadlines.md:49-55` (same company as SRE); twitter/finagle@ca472de `doc/src/sphinx/Contexts.rst:69-72` (`Deadline.current` "when the request should be completed by"); Azure/azure-sdk@4f33d3e `docs/general/azurecore.md:108` ("update any query parameter or request header … how much time the service has"). Edge (the first service starts the deadline): SRE "a deadline is set high in the stack" |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#54) |
| CC15 | code/references/concurrency.md | Claim each idempotency key atomically with its request fingerprint, store the response; replay it on a repeat, answer 409 while the first runs and 422 for another payload. | because otherwise a retry repeats the effect | ietf-wg-httpapi/idempotency@a2cd12a `draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header.md:121-133` (enforcement), `:160` (422 for a reused key with a different payload), `:183` (409 while the original is processed); brandur/sorg@3f7daa8 `content/articles/idempotency-keys.md:286` (`response_body` stored), `:419` (unique key constraint), `:610-611` (parameters mismatch rejected; Brandur answers 409, the rule follows the IETF draft's 422) |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: the 422 for another payload has the IETF draft alone; the rest has both sources. 2026-11-06 (#54) |
| CC16 | code/references/concurrency.md | Retry a 4xx response only when it carries `Retry-After`, and wait at least that long. | because other 4xx responses fail the same way again and an early retry ignores the server's request to wait | httpwg/http-core@bde37f6 `draft-ietf-httpbis-semantics-latest.xml:5770` (RFC 9110 §10.2.3: "how long the user agent ought to wait before making a follow-up request"); Azure/azure-sdk@4f33d3e `docs/general/azurecore.md:116` ("MUST NOT retry if the service responds with a 400-level response code unless a retry-after header is also returned") |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: "only when it carries `Retry-After`" has Azure alone; "wait at least that long" has both sources. 2026-11-06 (#54) |

## Concurrent edits (#75)

CC17–CC19 for `code/references/concurrency.md`, from the light research for #75. PS13 already holds the `@Version` mapping, and CC9 and CC15 hold the idempotency key on each side, so these three cover what reaches the client. Microsoft's Azure guidelines and Azure SDK count as one source. Operator decisions 2026-11-06: all three admitted; CC17's 428 and CC19's requirement and 400 are the operator's preference (Zalando alone here; RFC 6585 was not reachable to read); "never retry a user's edit automatically after a lock conflict" is left out (no source).

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC17 | code/references/concurrency.md | Send a versioned resource's `@Version` as its `ETag` and change it only under a matching `If-Match`; answer 412 on a mismatch and 428 when it is missing. | because otherwise the last write silently wins | zalando/restful-api-guidelines@19a1905 `chapters/best-practices.adoc:148-202` ("`ETag` with `If-Match` header" → "412 Precondition failed"), `chapters/http-headers.adoc:186-200`, `chapters/http-status-codes-and-errors.adoc:421-425` (428 "to make sure that the 'lost update problem' is avoided"); microsoft/api-guidelines@1c58ca7 `azure/Guidelines.md:174` ("DO support … optimistic concurrency by honoring the `If-Match` … headers"), `azure/ConsiderationsForServiceDesign.md:604-610`; spring-projects/spring-data-rest@61bb363 `src/main/antora/modules/ROOT/pages/etags-and-other-conditionals.adoc:20-33` (`@Version` served as the `ETag`; `If-Match` on `PUT`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`) |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: the 428 for a missing `If-Match` has Zalando alone; the rest has three sources. 2026-11-06 (#75) |
| CC18 | code/references/concurrency.md | Map an optimistic-lock failure (`OptimisticLockingFailureException`) to 409 Conflict in the global handler, never 500. | because the client lost a race and must reload, and a 500 reports it as a server fault | httpwg/http-core@bde37f6 `draft-ietf-httpbis-semantics-latest.xml:9476-9492` (RFC 9110 §15.5.10: "if versioning were being used and the representation being PUT included changes to a resource that conflict with those made by an earlier (third-party) request"); zalando/restful-api-guidelines@19a1905 `chapters/http-status-codes-and-errors.adoc:340-347` ("a version control conflict"); spring-projects/spring-data-rest@61bb363 `spring-data-rest-webmvc/src/main/java/org/springframework/data/rest/webmvc/RepositoryRestExceptionHandler.java:135-140` (`OptimisticLockingFailureException` → `HttpStatus.CONFLICT`) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#75) |
| CC19 | code/references/concurrency.md | Require an idempotency key header on every POST that creates or triggers work, and answer 400 when it is missing. | because a client's retry of a keyless POST creates the work twice | ietf-wg-httpapi/idempotency@a2cd12a `draft-ietf-httpapi-idempotency-key-header.md:138` ("If the `Idempotency-Key` request header is missing for a documented idempotent operation requiring this header, the resource SHOULD reply with an HTTP `400`"); microsoft/api-guidelines@1c58ca7 `azure/Guidelines.md:138` ("*all* service operations (including POST) **must** be idempotent"), `:146` (`Repeatability-Request-ID` is "one way"), `:536` ("DO support the Repeatability-Request-ID … headers") |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: requiring the key on every POST and the 400 when it is missing are the operator's preference (the IETF draft alone gives the 400, for an operation that already requires the header; Azure asks only that POST be idempotent and that the header be supported). 2026-11-06 (#75) |

(a small bug became 4 stories and 16 acceptance criteria) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
