| LN4 | crq/references/lanes.md | Count only an approval the agent cannot produce, a human forge action or a prompt only the human answers, recorded with the spec's hash. | because an agent-made approval passes its own work | GitHub docs, Copilot coding agent risks and mitigations https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/coding-agent/risks-and-mitigations (the agent "cannot approve or merge"; the requester cannot approve their own Copilot pull request); Google Jules docs https://jules.google/docs/ ("You can review and approve it before any code changes are made": the plan is approved in Jules' own UI, not on the forge) | agent-factory#30 gap 1, review issue 3 | APPROVED 2026-11-06; amended 2026-11-06 (operator: a harness prompt counts; recorded with the spec hash, since a shared login proves nothing) |

## crq pipeline: review process (skills#210, group B) — operator 2026-11-06: "all groups yes"

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RV1 | crq/references/review-process.md | Run every review as a find pass over every review check, then a fresh verify pass that re-opens each finding's evidence before anything is posted. | because one pass either misses findings or posts false ones | Claude Code docs, Code Review (code.claude.com/docs/en/code-review: Find → Verify → Rank → Post; "A verification step checks the candidate findings against the code and filters out false positives"); anthropics/claude-plugins-official `plugins/code-review/commands/code-review.md:56` @1aa4a4b (a validation subagent per finding); BitsAI-CR arXiv 2501.15134 Table 3 (filter stage, precision 60% → 75%) | agent-factory ADR-0004 | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| RV2 | crq/references/review-process.md | Post a finding only when the change introduced it, shows at `file:line`, can be acted on and the author would fix it. | because marginal findings hide real ones and slow every merge | openai/codex `codex-rs/prompts/templates/review/rubric.md:12-19,36` @a142c90 ("introduced in the commit", "author would likely fix", "prefer outputting no findings"); anthropics/claude-plugins-official `plugins/code-review/commands/code-review.md:42-52,80-87` @1aa4a4b (false-positive list: pre-existing, linter-catchable, nitpicks); Beko study arXiv 2412.18531 §IV-C3 (PR closure 5h52m → 8h20m with a noisy reviewer) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| RV3 | crq/references/review-process.md | Give the reviewer the approved spec and design, the full code at the head and the references in scope, never the writer's account. | because a reviewer that reads the writer's claims grades the claims | obra/superpowers `skills/subagent-driven-development/task-reviewer-prompt.md:64-71` @22f0b46 ("Do Not Trust the Report"); addyosmani/agent-skills `skills/doubt-driven-development/SKILL.md:93-100` @301b02b ("Do NOT pass the CLAIM"); Prometheus arXiv 2310.08491 Table 6 (removing the reference answer hurt most); MAST arXiv 2503.13657 (FM-3.2 incomplete verification) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| RV4 | crq/references/review-process.md | Report at most five nits in a review and never block on one. | because a long list of nits hides the findings that matter | Claude Code docs, Code Review customize page ("Report at most five Nits per review"); google/eng-practices `review/reviewer/standard.md:30-53` @3bb3ec2 ("Nit:", improvement over perfection) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| RV5 | crq/references/review-process.md | Log each finding by check, with its bucket and the human's action, and rewrite or retire a check its log shows is noise. | because without the log nobody can tell a useful check from noise | Google AutoCommenter arXiv 2405.13565 §5.1 (useful 54% → 66% after suppressing 17 rules); BitsAI-CR arXiv 2501.15134 §5 (rules removed from data); microsoft/code-with-engineering-playbook `docs/code-reviews/evidence-and-measures/README.md` @016770e; github/docs `content/copilot/tutorials/customize-code-review.md` @17d6ee5 (add instructions one at a time, test on real PRs) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| RV6 | crq/references/review-process.md | Let a review block a change but never approve it, and read zero findings as no signal. | because a reviewer that a code comment talks into "no findings" would pass any change | GitHub docs, Copilot code review https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/agents/code-review (reviews "do not count toward required approvals" by default; an approval is dismissed on a new push); Spotify engineering https://engineering.atspotify.com/2025/12/feedback-loops-background-coding-agents-part-3 (the LLM judge vetoes about a quarter of sessions; one layer before engineers review) | agent-factory#83 gap 6 | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |

| VB1 | crq/references/verify-done.md | When the spec does not settle a question, post it as an open question and stop; never guess an acceptance criterion. | because a guessed criterion ships the wrong behaviour with passing tests | MAST arXiv 2503.13657 (FM-2.2 failing to ask for clarification, 6.8% of failures); github/spec-kit `templates/commands/specify.md:128-133` @3ca735b (`[NEEDS CLARIFICATION]` markers); Kiro https://kiro.dev/docs/specs/analyze-requirements/ (ambiguities and unstated assumptions raised as questions) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| VB2 | crq/references/verify-done.md | Give each changed behaviour a test that fails when the change is reverted. | because a test that passes either way proves nothing about the change | google/eng-practices `review/reviewer/looking-for.md:63-76` @3bb3ec2 ("Will the tests actually fail when the code is broken?"); addyosmani/agent-skills `skills/code-review-and-quality/SKILL.md:157-164` @301b02b (invert one condition; a green mutant is a finding); obra/superpowers `skills/verification-before-completion/SKILL.md:84` @22f0b46 (revert the fix, see the test fail, restore) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| VB3 | crq/references/verify-done.md | Call a change ready only when its build, lint and touched tests pass at the pushed commit, checked by a script. | because a writer's "done" is a report, not proof | kunchenguid/firstmate `fm-dod-lib.sh:533` @8b1618a (done accepted only on the pushed HEAD); obra/superpowers `skills/verification-before-completion/SKILL.md:17-48` @22f0b46 ("NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE"); Anthropic, Prompting Claude Opus 5.5 (platform.claude.com: "Treat a text-only end of turn as a report rather than as proof"); Jain https://martinfowler.com/articles/are-harnesses-worth-the-cost.html (completion from recorded observations, not the agent's claim) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| VB4 | crq/references/verify-done.md | For each acceptance criterion a user can see, drive the running app and paste what it returned. | because passing tests can still leave the feature broken for the user | Anthropic engineering, harness design for long-running apps (anthropic.com: an evaluator drives the app with Playwright); Factory-AI/factory `docs/missions/overview.mdx:37` @892925c (QA needs a scriptable way to exercise the app); cursor/plugins `pstack/skills/create-verification-skill/SKILL.md` @2776ddd; gemini-cli-extensions/conductor `workflow.md:113` @6e8f9a8 (manual verification plan per phase) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| VB5 | crq/references/verify-done.md | Map every acceptance criterion to its evidence in the pull request (criterion, command, result). | because a criterion with no evidence is unproved | addyosmani/agent-skills `skills/shipping-and-launch/references/definition-of-done.md:22-51` @301b02b; obra/superpowers `skills/verification-before-completion/SKILL.md` @22f0b46 (requirements met → line-by-line checklist); METR blog 2025-08-12 (0 of 15 test-passing agent PRs mergeable as-is) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| VB6 | crq/references/verify-done.md | Never delete, skip or weaken a test to reach green; fix the code, or ask. | because a weakened test hides the defect it was written to catch | Anthropic engineering, effective harnesses for long-running agents ("It is unacceptable to remove or edit tests"); github/docs `review-ai-generated-code.md:80-86` @17d6ee5 (tests deleted or skipped instead of fixed); Jain are-harnesses-worth-the-cost ("correct the implementation … rather than weaken the expectations"); Anthropic prompting best practices ("inform me rather than working around them") | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| VB7 | crq/references/verify-done.md | For a bug fix, have a separate run write the failing test from the bug spec before the fix is written. | because a writer that tests its own fix shares its misreading | AgentCoder arXiv 2312.13010 Table 7 (one agent for code and tests: pass@1 71.3% vs 79.9%, test accuracy 61.0% vs 87.8%); Agentless arXiv 2407.01489 §5.1.3 (only 94 of 213 agent reproduction tests passed with the real fix); S4 | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CR1 | crq/references/code-review.md | Check the diff against the approved spec and design, and report each criterion missing, each behaviour nobody asked for and each requirement misread. | because code can pass every test and still build the wrong thing | obra/superpowers `skills/subagent-driven-development/task-reviewer-prompt.md:98-103` @22f0b46 (Missing / Extra / Misunderstood); bmad-code-org/BMAD-METHOD `customize.toml:137` @022be93 (intent-alignment lens); mattpocock/skills `skills/engineering/code-review/SKILL.md:6-80` @4ae2317 (Spec axis); humanlayer/humanlayer `apps/riptide-daemon/src/agents/claudecode/agents/implementation-reviewer.ts:47-90` @f9bd7353 (as planned, deviations, additions, missing); Jeffallan/claude-skills `skills/code-reviewer/references/spec-compliance-review.md:93` @078a25c | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| CR2 | crq/references/code-review.md | Keep a change within about 400 changed lines and 15 files, and split it beyond 20 files, not counting generated files. | because review usefulness falls as a change grows | Czerwonka, Greiler, Tilford ICSE-SEIP 2015 (microsoft.com: comment usefulness drops noticeably from 20+ files); google/eng-practices `review/developer/small-cls.md:65-66` @3bb3ec2 (about 100 lines is reasonable, 1000 is usually too large); Sadowski et al. ICSE-SEIP 2018 §5.2 (median change 24 lines; ~90% touch fewer than 10 files); addyosmani/agent-skills `skills/code-review-and-quality/SKILL.md:107-111` @301b02b | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 |
| CR3 | crq/references/code-review.md | Send a change to CI config, build files, a new dependency, code-owner files or agent instruction files (`.agents/` listed in the reference) to the human. | because an agent that edits its own gate, rules or dependencies can pass any change | github/gh-aw `docs/src/content/docs/reference/threat-detection.md:543-588` @b78fa77 (protected files: manifests, `.github/`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/`, `CODEOWNERS` → REQUEST_CHANGES); github/docs `about-third-party-coding-agents.md:87-95` and `about-automations.md:168-171` @17d6ee5 (agent PRs get no CI run until a person with write access approves) | | APPROVED 2026-11-06; amended 2026-11-06 (new dependency, `.agents/`; operator, agent-factory#30 Q5b) |
| CR4 | crq/references/code-review.md | Give each raised threshold or disabled check in a config file its reason on the line above it. | because a silent exception hides the problem the check was built to catch | Böckeler https://martinfowler.com/articles/sensors-for-coding-agents.html (the suppressions and raised thresholds an agent created are the entry points for review); CM7 | | APPROVED 2026-11-06 · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part |

## API design (#74)

AP1–AP6 for the new `code/references/api-design.md`, from the light research for #74. PS4–PS6 (`persistence.md`) hold the query behind a page. Sources: zalando/restful-api-guidelines@19a1905 (`chapters/pagination.adoc`, `chapters/compatibility.adoc`), aip-dev/google.aip.dev@2ccc61a (`aip/general/0158.md`, `0180.md`, `0185.md`), microsoft/api-guidelines@1c58ca7 (`azure/Guidelines.md`). Operator decisions 2026-11-06: AP3's names `items` and `next` are Zalando's (the three sources name the one page shape differently); AP6 takes Google's major version in the path, where Azure uses an `api-version` query parameter and Zalando says "MUST not use URL versioning" (`compatibility.adoc:307`).

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AP1 | code/references/api-design.md | Paginate every list endpoint from its first release, with a documented default and maximum page size, and cap a larger request at the maximum. | because adding pagination later breaks clients that read the whole list | AIP-158 `0158.md:20-22` ("must provide pagination *at the outset*, as it is a backwards-incompatible change"), `:60-67` (default; "coerce down to the maximum permitted page size"), `:150-176`; Azure `Guidelines.md:548-550` ("It is a breaking change to add paging in the future"), `:753` (`maxpagesize` "with a default value"), `:755` ("may choose to return fewer resources"); Zalando `pagination.adoc:6-11` ("MUST support pagination", for lists larger than a few hundred entries). Edge (in-memory page for a small collection): AIP-158 `:174-176` |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#74) |
| AP2 | code/references/api-design.md | Page with an opaque cursor that the client returns unchanged, absent on the last page, not an offset. | because an offset skips or repeats rows when data changes and slows on deep pages | Zalando `pagination.adoc:35-66` ("prefer cursor-based pagination, avoid offset-based pagination"; "an opaque pointer to a page, that must never be *inspected* or *constructed* by clients"); AIP-158 `0158.md:88` (empty token on the last page), `:120` ("must be opaque"); Azure `Guidelines.md:563-581` (opaque `nextLink`; "DO NOT return the `nextLink` field at all when returning the last page"). Edge (jump to page N): Zalando `pagination.adoc:46-48`. AIP-158 `0158.md:99-116` and Azure `Guidelines.md:593` permit a `skip` offset. Edge (PS5 for pages that never reach an API client): scope of the rule |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: "not an offset" has Zalando alone (a SHOULD; Google and Azure permit `skip`); the opaque cursor absent on the last page has three sources. 2026-11-06 (#74) |
| AP3 | code/references/api-design.md | Return every page in one shape across the API, `items` plus a `next` cursor. | because a different shape per endpoint makes every client write its own pager | Zalando `pagination.adoc:69-90` (one "response page object": `next`, `items`); Azure `Guidelines.md:546` ("an object with a top-level array field"), `:555-581` (`value` and `nextLink`); AIP-158 `0158.md:81-91` (`next_page_token` on every list response) |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: one shape has three sources; the names `items` and `next` are the operator's choice of Zalando's. 2026-11-06 (#74) |
| AP4 | code/references/api-design.md | Change a published API only compatibly: add optional fields; never remove, rename or retype a field, change its meaning or make an optional input required. | because clients built on the old contract break | Zalando `compatibility.adoc:6-11` ("MUST not break backward compatibility"), `:41-60` (`:48` "Never change the semantic of fields"; "Add optional fields, but never mandatory fields"; "Don't remove fields"; `:57` "Make mandatory fields optional, but not vice-versa"); AIP-180 `0180.md:63` ("New required fields must not be added"), `:89-99` (components "must not be removed … in the same major version"; a rename adds the new one and keeps the old), `:115-119` ("must not have their type changed"), `:152-160` ("must not change visible behavior or semantics") |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: "never retype" has AIP-180 alone and "never make an optional input required" has Zalando alone; add, remove, rename and meaning have two sources. 2026-11-06 (#74) |
| AP5 | code/references/api-design.md | Make each client of another service ignore unknown response fields and map an unknown enum value to a fallback. | because a compatible addition on the server otherwise breaks the client | Zalando `compatibility.adoc:150-169` ("MUST prepare clients to accept compatible API extensions"; "Be tolerant with unknown fields"; for enums "provide default behavior for unknown values"; keep both for a later PUT); Azure `Guidelines.md:435-442` (extensible enums: "undocumented value may returned in the future"). Edge (keep unknown fields and enum strings for PUT): Zalando `compatibility.adoc:163-169` |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: "ignore unknown response fields" has Zalando alone; the enum fallback has two sources. 2026-11-06 (#74) |
| AP6 | code/references/api-design.md | Put the major version in every path from the first release (`/v1/…`); add `/v2` only for a change AP4 forbids. | because clients and gateways route on the URL, and a late version moves every URL | AIP-185 `0185.md:17-21` ("must provide a *major version number*", "included as the first part of the URI path"; an incompatible change follows AIP-180); Zalando `compatibility.adoc:223-230` (a new version only when a change "can't be done in a compatible way"). Placement in the path from the first release: Google only; Azure uses `api-version`; Zalando forbids URL versioning (`:307`) and stays "without versioning" until an incompatible change (`:297-298`) |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: the path placement and "from the first release" are the operator's choice among disagreeing sources; "a new major version only for an incompatible change" has two. 2026-11-06 (#74) |

## Scheduling and batch jobs (#52)

SJ1–SJ7 for the new `code/references/scheduling.md`, from the light research for #52. MS6 (`messaging.md`) already makes a job safe to run twice, MXN3 (`metrics.md`) gives it a last-success gauge and an in-flight timer, SS16 checks its authorization, PS9 and PS10 cover bulk updates and JDBC batching, and CC8 and LGN1 cover retries and log output. Production evidence read for this set: danluu/post-mortems@630562e `README.md` (one-paragraph summaries; the primary pages were blocked from the session): `:357` (Google: a batch cron emailed people more than 20 times because it marked them done only after the whole run; MS6), `:125` (Travis CI: an age-based image cleanup job deleted the stable base images; SJ7), `:349` (GitHub, July 2024: a partner's scheduled cleanup job removed Copilot infrastructure, 19h26m to restore; SJ7), `:487` (Tarsnap: a batch job retried failing deletes forever and logged to local disk until it filled; CC8, LGN1). Operator decisions 2026-11-06: all seven admitted; SJ7 is the operator's preference (one practice source plus incidents); a cap on deletions per run is left out (no source read).

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SJ1 | code/references/scheduling.md | Guard each scheduled job on a multi-instance service with a cluster lock (ShedLock, a database lock) or a CronJob with `concurrencyPolicy: Forbid`. | because every instance otherwise runs it at once | lukas-krecan/ShedLock@90dbd20 `README.md:5` ("makes sure that your scheduled tasks are executed at most once at the same time"); kubernetes/website@380372f `content/en/docs/concepts/workloads/controllers/cron-jobs.md:135` (`Forbid` "does not allow concurrent runs"); Google SRE book, "Distributed Periodic Scheduling with Cron", Cron Jobs and Idempotency ("we favor skipping launches rather than risking double launches … undoing a double launch … may be difficult or even entirely impossible") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#52) |
| SJ2 | code/references/scheduling.md | Set ShedLock's `lockAtMostFor` well above the job's longest run. | because a job that outlives its lock runs twice at once | lukas-krecan/ShedLock@90dbd20 `README.md:121-124` ("You have to set `lockAtMostFor` to a value which is much longer than normal execution time … more than one process will effectively hold the lock"), `:126-127` (`lockAtLeastFor` "to prevent execution from multiple nodes in case of really short tasks and clock difference between the nodes"). Vendor docs on the vendor's API (decision V) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (vendor docs, decision V, #52) |
| SJ3 | code/references/scheduling.md | Process a large set in chunks, each committed in its own transaction, never one transaction over every row. | because one long transaction grows the persistence context and loses all work on failure | spring-projects/spring-batch@84bc358 `spring-batch-docs/modules/ROOT/pages/step/chunk-oriented-processing.adoc:4-8` ("chunks that are written out within a transaction boundary … then the transaction is committed"); hibernate/hibernate-orm@db0517b `documentation/src/main/asciidoc/userguide/chapters/batch/Batching.adoc:84-95` (one large context "can eventually cause an `OutOfMemoryError`"; "Use `flush()` … and `clear()`"); Google SRE book, "Data Processing Pipelines" (no checkpointing restarts every chunk from the beginning) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#52) |
| SJ4 | code/references/scheduling.md | Give the task scheduler more than one thread, or virtual threads, once a service has a second scheduled job. | because Spring's default scheduler has one thread and a slow job delays the others | spring-projects/spring-boot@ae7fcd8 `documentation/spring-boot-docs/src/docs/antora/modules/reference/pages/features/task-execution-and-scheduling.adoc:118-122` (`ThreadPoolTaskScheduler` "uses one thread by default"; virtual threads use `SimpleAsyncTaskScheduler`). Vendor docs on the vendor's API (decision V) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (vendor docs, decision V, #52) |
| SJ5 | code/references/scheduling.md | Name the time zone of every cron schedule (`zone = "UTC"` on `@Scheduled`, `.spec.timeZone` on a CronJob). | because the default is the scheduler's or host's zone, so the run time depends on where it runs | spring-projects/spring-framework@07c005f `framework-docs/modules/ROOT/pages/integration/scheduling.adoc:350` ("use the `zone` attribute to specify the time zone"), `spring-context/src/main/java/org/springframework/scheduling/annotation/Scheduled.java:118-127` ("By default … the scheduler's time zone will be used"); kubernetes/website@380372f `cron-jobs.md:182-187` ("interprets schedules relative to its local time zone"; `.spec.timeZone: "Etc/UTC"`) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#52) |
| SJ6 | code/references/scheduling.md | Schedule a job at a minute other than 0, such as 03:17. | because jobs that many teams start at minute 0 load shared systems together and are delayed or dropped | Google SRE book, "Distributed Periodic Scheduling with Cron", Thundering herd ("When people think of a 'daily cron job,' they commonly configure this job to run at midnight"; hashed start times); github/docs@17d6ee5 `data/reusables/actions/schedule-delay.md:1` ("High load times include the start of every hour … some queued jobs may be dropped … schedule your workflow to run at a different time of the hour") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#52) |
| SJ7 | code/references/scheduling.md | In a job that deletes data, soft-delete first and destroy only after a delay. | because a wrong filter otherwise destroys everything it matches before anyone notices | Google SRE book, "Data Integrity" (deletion by software bugs is the most common user-visible data loss; soft deletion "has proven to be an effective defense"; lazy deletion keeps data "for up to a few weeks before destruction"; edge: lazy deletion is impractical where destruction must be guaranteed in time) | danluu/post-mortems `README.md:125` (Travis CI image cleanup), `:349` (GitHub July 2024 cleanup job) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: one practice source (Google SRE) plus two incidents as evidence. 2026-11-06 (#52) |

## Incident check of #54, #57, #74 and #75

A check of the merged PS, CC and AP rules against production incidents, not documentation. Incidents read: AWS postmortems at aws.amazon.com/message (primary: 5467D2 DynamoDB Sep 2015, 65648 EBS Apr 2011, 65649 SimpleDB Jun 2014, 12721 Dec 2021, 101925 DynamoDB DNS Oct 2025); Google SRE workbook "Managing Load" (Pokémon GO, Jul 2016) and "Postmortem Culture" (satellite disk erase) (primary); danluu/post-mortems@630562e `README.md` and icco/postmortems@9354703 `data/*.md` (summaries; the original pages were blocked from the session). Incidents are evidence; they confirm that CC3, CC4, CC8, CC9, CC10, CC11, CC12, CC15, CC17, PS2, PS6, PS13, AP4, AP5 and MG36 are needed. Operator decisions 2026-11-06: admit CC13 (reversing the #54 decision), CC20, AP7 and PS15; widen CC19 (requests and messages; jobs stay under MS6) and MG36. AP5 now says every use handles the enum's fallback (the Chrome Sync evidence of AP7). Held for more evidence (one summarized incident each): read every page before acting on what a list omits (Travis CI, icco `f1af2841`), keep transactions narrow (incident.io, `README.md:227`), record intent before charging (Twilio, icco `4fc691c5`). Note: ledger rows AP1–AP5, AP3a and AP4a above (`.agents/skills/code/references/api.md`) are agent-platform's own rules, not plugin rules; the plugin's AP ids are those of `code/references/api-design.md`.

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CC13 | code/references/concurrency.md | Draw every retry from a client-wide retry budget of about 10% of requests, beside the per-call cap, and stop retrying when it is spent. | because per-call caps still multiply load in an outage | Google SRE book, "Handling Overload" `sre-book_handling-overload:230` ("per-client retry budget (a 10% retry ratio)"); twitter/finagle@ca472de `doc/src/sphinx/Clients.rst:272` ("`RetryBudget` that allows for approximately 20% of the total requests to be retried") | SRE workbook "Managing Load" `:157` (Pokémon GO: "effectively synchronize client retries … 20× the previous global RPS peak", primary); AWS 5467D2 (DynamoDB 2015, pausing requests "decreased retry activity", primary); AWS 12721 ("even more connection attempts and retries", primary); AWS 65648 (EBS 2011, nodes "did not back off aggressively enough", primary); icco `aa0709c9` (GitHub May 2023, a caller "that retried on timeouts", summary); icco `43164f6c` (LaunchDarkly Oct 2025, "excessive retries from SDKs", summary) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: the ~10% figure is Google's alone (Finagle allows about 20%); a client-wide budget has two sources. 2026-11-06 (incident check; reverses the #54 decision) |
| CC19 | code/references/concurrency.md | Require an idempotency key on every request or message that creates or triggers work, and reject one without it (400 over HTTP). | because a repeated request or message otherwise repeats the work | Supersedes the #75 row (POST only). Edge (a natural key claimed with the change counts as the key): MS6's sources. Idempotent commands beyond HTTP: SRE workbook "Postmortem Culture" `:301` ("All commands should be idempotent, or at least fail-safe on repeat invocations"); microsoft/api-guidelines@1c58ca7 `azure/Guidelines.md:138` ("*all* service operations (including POST) **must** be idempotent"); the 400 and the key: as in the #75 row | SRE workbook "Postmortem Culture" `:282` (a non-idempotent procedure re-run erased every satellite's disks, primary); danluu `README.md:495` (Twilio repeated charges, summary) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: requiring a key and rejecting a command without it remain the operator's preference; idempotent commands have two sources. 2026-11-06 (incident check) |
| CC20 | code/references/concurrency.md | Set each remote timeout from the call's measured p99 latency with headroom, and give an optional call a short timeout and a fallback. | because a guessed timeout cuts healthy calls or holds threads | No practice source read; evidence only | AWS 5467D2 (metadata responses "exceeded the retrieval and transmission time allowed", too short, primary; the Console's "very long timeout … The API call did not have to complete … It should have simply failed quickly", too long, primary); AWS 65649 (SimpleDB 2014: "hindsight shows the value was too low", primary); icco `3d2adf6c` (Fortnite Feb 2018, workers waiting on Memcached timeouts, summary) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: the whole rule rests on three incidents (four cases) and no practice source. Edge (a shorter remaining deadline wins): CC14's sources. 2026-11-06 (incident check) |
| AP7 | code/references/api-design.md | Skip and log a record in another service's response that the client cannot read, never fail the whole batch. | because one unreadable record otherwise stops every record behind it | No practice source read beyond AP5's; evidence only | icco `91fb1613` (Chrome Sync Dec 2012: an unknown enum "returned an `UNSPECIFIED` value, causing an `out_of_range` … crash", summary); danluu `README.md:23` (CircleCI Nov 2021: the distributor failed on every scan of rows it could not read, summary) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: two summarized incidents, no practice source. 2026-11-06 (incident check) |
| MG36 | migrations/references/postgres-design.md | Never declare `serial`, `integer` keys or key references, `char(n)` or `json`; use `bigint GENERATED … AS IDENTITY` (or `uuid`), `text` and `jsonb`. | because int keys overflow, char(n) pads, json lacks equality, and serial complicates permissions | Supersedes the earlier MG36 row; adds key references | danluu `README.md:215` (GitHub May 2021: a foreign key "hit max INT32", summary); icco `a532970a` (Heroku Jun 2023: "a foreign key used a smaller data type than the primary key that it referenced", summary) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: "key references" rests on two summarized incidents and no practice source; the rest keeps its earlier sources. 2026-11-06 (incident check) |
| PS15 | code/references/persistence.md | Stop a bulk update or delete whose target list is empty before it runs; never let an empty list mean no filter. | because an empty filter applies the command to every row | No practice source read; evidence only | SRE workbook "Postmortem Culture" `:282-301` (an empty list of targets erased every satellite's disks; "a missing safety check", primary) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: one primary incident, no practice source. 2026-11-06 (incident check) |

## Redis and Redis Streams (#71, #56)

RD1–RD6 for the new `code/references/redis.md` (#71) and MS7–MS12 in `code/references/messaging.md` (#56), from the light research for both. Redis's docs (redis/docs@875468d), its agent skills (redis/agent-skills@74708da) and Lettuce count as one source (Redis). Independent sources: spring-projects/spring-data-redis@88b4366 (`src/main/antora/modules/ROOT/pages/redis/`), the AWS ElastiCache client best-practices post (aws.amazon.com/blogs/database/best-practices-redis-clients-and-amazon-elasticache-for-redis/). Already covered: S1 and the IT rules (Redis tested with a real container), CC3, CC8 and CC13 (timeouts and retries on Redis calls, including Square's WATCH/MULTI retry storm, icco `d6db1e76`), MS4, MS6, MSN1. Production evidence (all summaries; the primary write-ups were blocked): Twilio, Jul 2013 (icco `4fc691c5`, danluu `README.md:495`: resync storm after a partition, master restarted read-only, "loss of all in-flight account balance data"); Discord, Oct 2017 (danluu `README.md:317`, icco `a46ebba4`: "known issues with the way Discord API instances handle Redis failover"); GitHub, Mar 2026 (icco `e696c413`: "a Redis cluster failover … leaving the cluster without a writable primary"); GitHub, Jan 2016 (icco `5d85d316`: "a hard dependency in the application's boot path on Redis clusters"); GitHub, Sep 2012 (icco `402b4363`: MySQL split-brain "caused inconsistencies between MySQL and Redis"). No incident was found for KEYS, TTLs, Streams or pub/sub. Operator decisions 2026-11-06: all twelve admitted; RD3, RD5, RD6 and MS8 are operator preference, and so are RD2's jitter and MS11's group creation (found by the review); cache-stampede protection is left out (no Redis source or Redis incident read).

| id | reference | rule | reason | source | evidence | status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RD1 | code/references/redis.md | Never run `KEYS`, or `SMEMBERS`, `HGETALL` or `LRANGE 0 -1` on a large collection; iterate with `SCAN`, `SSCAN`, `HSCAN` or `ZSCAN` and a `COUNT`, or a bounded `LRANGE`. | because one O(N) command blocks all clients | redis/docs `content/commands/keys.md:63` ("Use extreme care when using this command in production"), `content/operate/oss_and_stack/management/optimization/latency.md:215-216`; spring-data-redis `redis-cache.adoc:73-74` ("defaults to use `KEYS` … can cause performance issues with large keyspaces"; `SCAN`-based `BatchStrategy`); redis/agent-skills `skills/redis-connections/SKILL.md:57-66` (Redis); AWS ElastiCache client post, large composite items ("the client can do a HGETALL command to retrieve the entire multi GB hash collection … use HGET or HMGET instead of HGETALL") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#71) |
| RD2 | code/references/redis.md | Give every cache entry a TTL, with jitter for entries written together. | because entries without one fill memory until eviction drops what you need, and mass expiry blocks the server | redis/docs `latency.md:554-571` (latency generated by expires; many keys expiring in the same second); spring-data-redis `redis-cache.adoc:169-184` (TTL expiration, `entryTtl`); docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonElastiCache/latest/dg/Strategies.html, "Adding TTL" | icco `e696c413` (GitHub Feb 2026: a cache TTL change overwhelmed the user database; backend not named, not counted) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: the jitter for entries written together rests on Redis alone (`latency.md:554-571`); a TTL on every cache entry has three sources. 2026-11-06 (#71) |
| RD3 | code/references/redis.md | Keep the system of record outside Redis, and let the service start and degrade without it. | because a failover or restart can lose recent writes | redis/docs `content/develop/data-types/streams/_index.md:929` (failover "performs only a *best effort* check … may promote a replica that lacks some data") | Twilio 2013 (icco `4fc691c5`), GitHub 2016 (icco `5d85d316`), GitHub 2012 (icco `402b4363`) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: one practice source (Redis) plus three summarized incidents. 2026-11-06 (#71) |
| RD4 | code/references/redis.md | Share one multiplexed Lettuce connection or a bounded pool (CC10), never a connection per call, and give blocking reads their own connection. | because a blocking command stalls every request that shares it | redis/agent-skills `skills/redis-connections/SKILL.md:22-32` ("never one connection per request"; multiplexing "**cannot** carry blocking commands like `BLPOP`") (Redis); AWS ElastiCache client post ("Use a connection pool"; "a client connection pool with a finite size"; persistent connections); spring-data-redis `drivers.adoc:137-138` (the shared native connection carries "all non-blocking and non-transactional operations"; a pool for blocking ones) |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#71) |
| RD5 | code/references/redis.md | Reconnect with jittered backoff, retry an idempotent command (CC8) on `READONLY` or a connection error, and test failover with a proxy cut (TM4). | because clients that mishandle failover take the service down | AWS ElastiCache client post ("fires several reconnection and discovery requests without adding exponential backoff upon retry"; "retry with exponential backoff with jitter") | Discord 2017 (danluu `README.md:317`), GitHub Mar 2026 (icco `e696c413`), Twilio 2013 (icco `4fc691c5`) | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: one practice source plus three summarized incidents; the failover test follows TM4. 2026-11-06 (#71) |
| RD6 | code/references/redis.md | Name keys in lowercase, colon-separated, with a service or tenant prefix (`billing:order:42:lines`). | because mixed schemes make scans, ACLs and cleanup per prefix impossible | redis/docs `content/develop/using-commands/keyspace.md:38-60` (colon convention, "object-type:id"); redis/agent-skills `skills/redis-core/SKILL.md:40-60` (lowercase, colon-separated, tenant prefix) (both Redis) |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: Redis is the only source. 2026-11-06 (#71) |
| MS7 | code/references/messaging.md | Read a stream consumer group without auto-ack (`receiveAutoAck`, `noack`, `NOACK`) and acknowledge per MSN1. | because an auto-acked message is lost if the consumer dies before it finishes | redis/docs `streams/_index.md:369`, `:394` (pending entries; `XACK` marks a message processed); spring-data-redis `redis-streams.adoc:166-189` ("Acknowledge strategies"; acknowledge "after processing"; `receiveAutoAck`). Narrows MSN1 for Streams |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#56) |
| MS8 | code/references/messaging.md | On start and on a timer, reclaim pending stream entries idle longer than the processing time (`XAUTOCLAIM`, or `XPENDING` and `XCLAIM`). | because a dead consumer's pending messages are otherwise never processed | redis/docs `streams/_index.md:552-560` (consume the pending history on restart; *claim* a dead consumer's pending messages), `:644-648` (`XAUTOCLAIM`) |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: Redis is the only source. 2026-11-06 (#56) |
| MS9 | code/references/messaging.md | Move a stream message whose delivery count passes a limit to a dead-letter stream and acknowledge it. | because a poison message is otherwise redelivered forever | redis/docs `streams/_index.md:683` ("once the deliveries counter reaches a given large number … put such messages in another stream … the *dead letter* concept"), `:703` (`XNACK … FATAL`); spring-kafka's dead-letter publishing after the retries are exhausted, as cited in the MS4 row |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#56) |
| MS10 | code/references/messaging.md | Cap every stream with `XADD … MAXLEN ~` or `MINID`. | because a stream keeps every entry until it is trimmed and fills memory | redis/docs `streams/_index.md:38` ("trimming strategies (to prevent streams from growing unbounded)"), `:840-868` (`MAXLEN`, `MAXLEN ~`), `:884` (`MINID`); spring-data-redis `redis-streams.adoc:15` ("retains messages until the stream is trimmed") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#56) |
| MS11 | code/references/messaging.md | Create a consumer group at `0` or a stored id, and read from the last consumed offset, never "latest". | because "latest" skips messages added between polls | spring-data-redis `redis-streams.adoc:205-212` ("Using the latest message for read can skip messages that were added to the stream while the poll operation was in the state of dead time"); redis/docs `streams/_index.md:445-448` (the `>` and explicit-id reads). Edge (live data that may be missed): scope of the rule |  | APPROVED · OPERATOR PREFERENCE (SRC2), in whole or in part: creating the group at `0` or a stored id has no source (the Redis docs create groups at `$`); reading from the last consumed offset has two. 2026-11-06 (#56) |
| MS12 | code/references/messaging.md | Never use Redis pub/sub for a message that must arrive; use a stream. | because pub/sub is fire-and-forget and drops a message no subscriber is listening for | redis/docs `streams/_index.md:296` ("in Pub/Sub messages are *fire and forget* and are never stored"); spring-data-redis `redis-streams.adoc:15` ("if you don't listen, you miss a message") |  | APPROVED 2026-11-06 (#56) |
